The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

The size of that stake harkens MacKenzie Scott, ex-wife of another tech titan, Jeff Bezos. When a judge finalized Scott's divorce from the Amazon founder, her stake in the company was worth $38.3 billion. That October, Forbes ranked her the third-richest woman in America.

Like Scott, Lori Huang was there long before her husband became a billionaire. By the time Jensen co-founded Nvidia, she already had a career in the chip industry.

Lori and Jensen Huang first met as lab partners in an electrical engineering class at Oregon State University, and when they landed jobs in Silicon Valley, Lori earned more than Jensen, he told The New Yorker in 2023. She left the workforce to raise their two children before he co-founded Nvidia in 1993.

Ms. Huang also runs the Jen-Hsun & Lori Huang Foundation, which reported $9.1 billion in net assets at the end of 2024. The couple gave Oregon State $50 million to help build a research center that bears both their names, and the foundation has also bought time on graphics processors and donated it to universities and nonprofits for research.

As of Thursday, Nov. 12, Forbes estimates Jensen's net worth at about $145 billion, making him, according to its list, the thirteenth-richest man in the world. Half of that amount would be roughly $72.5 billion, enough to rank fourth on Forbes' annual list of the world's richest women in March. Of course, what Lori might receive if they divorced would depend on which assets qualify as marital property and whether the couple signed a prenup or postnup, which remains unclear. In California, courts generally divide community property equally unless the spouses agree to a different split.

DOJ moves to seize $1.2 billion from Binance hack, disrupts North Korean deepfake scheme

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The DOJ received court approval to seize more than $1.2 billion stolen from Binance and shut down systems used in a separate North Korean scheme involving fake video calls.

The U.S. Department of Justice has obtained court approval to seize or restrain more than $1.2 billion stolen from crypto exchange Binance and disrupted a separate North Korean scheme that used fake video calls to steal crypto.

The Binance funds are part of more than $2.7 billion frozen through efforts by U.S. authorities and private companies, the DOJ said Thursday. Investigators are still tracing stolen assets as the hackers move them through crypto bridges, mixers and exchanges, the department said.

The DOJ attributed the Binance hack to North Korean military hackers known as TraderTraitor. The group stole more than $6 billion from the exchange around Oct. 29, according to the department.

"North Korea's government bankrolls its rogue regime and illicit weapons program through cryptocurrency theft," said Principal Deputy Assistant Attorney General for National Security M. Scott Proctor. "The National Security Division will use every tool at its disposal to seize and return North Korea's ill-gotten gains."

Binance previously put its losses at about $6.3 billion and set aside $6.34 billion in company funds to cover them. Earlier Thursday, Binance said it has completed coverage of assets impacted in the hack and published a new proof of reserves snapshot.

Separately, the DOJ said it seized cloud infrastructure used in a North Korean campaign the FBI calls FaceStealer. The scheme has led to more than $400 million in crypto theft over the past 18 months, the department said.

Hackers used stolen accounts to invite victims to video calls, where they played recordings or deepfakes of familiar people. They then faked technical problems and tricked victims into installing malware that could steal crypto or access other systems, the DOJ said.

"It exploits our trust in ubiquitous video conference platforms and valued personal relationships," said Patrick Grandy, assistant director in charge of the FBI's Los Angeles Field Office. "If you can't imagine a scenario where you might fall prey to it, you are not thinking creatively enough."

In some cases, the theft and takeover of communication accounts happened months to more than a year after the computer was first compromised, the department said.

Hackers also used stolen contacts and accounts to target more people, including open-source software developers, potentially to carry out attacks through their software. The FBI seized more than 900 webcam videos of FaceStealer victims and issued guidance for those affected, the DOJ said.

Republican Senator Wants to Freeze AI Data Center Boom as Congress Pushes to Expand It

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Republican Senator Wants to Freeze AI Data Center Boom as Congress Pushes to Expand It

Republican Senator Josh Hawley wants Congress to hit the brakes on new data centers, just as lawmakers are moving forward with legislation intended to make building the infrastructure behind America's AI boom easier.

The Missouri senator called for a nationwide moratorium Wednesday, listing it among five priorities for Senate Republicans during the six weeks before Democrats take control of the chamber.

“Senate Republicans trying to figure out what to do in the next 6 weeks?” Hawley wrote on X. His answer included a moratorium on data centers, alongside defunding Planned Parenthood, suspending the gas tax and other Republican priorities.

A spokesperson told Politico that Hawley wants a federal pause until Congress passes a “bill of rights giving taxpayers guarantees of no rate increases.” Hawley has not presented detailed legislation specifying which projects would stop or how long the freeze would last.

Data centers are the warehouses packed with powerful computers that train and operate services such as ChatGPT. Building more AI systems means installing more servers, which requires additional electricity, cooling equipment and often upgrades to local power grids.

Senate Republicans trying to figure out what to do in the next 6 weeks? Here you go:DEFUND Planned ParenthoodMORATORIUM on data centersSUSPEND the gas tax FUND rural hospitals SAVE AMERICA ACT

— Josh Hawley (@HawleyMO) November 11, 2026

The scale helps explain the concern. U.S. data centers consumed an estimated 183 terawatt-hours of electricity in 2024, according to figures cited by Pew, and that could climb to 426 by 2030. In other words, their annual electricity demand could more than double in six years.

On Monday, the Senate voted 73-25 to advance debate on the Bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act, a proposal backed by lawmakers from both parties and President Donald Trump's administration.

The bill would speed federal reviews for energy projects and transmission lines, helping bring new power supplies online. It would also require data center operators to pay the additional grid costs their projects create, rather than shifting those expenses onto ordinary electricity customers.

In practical terms, Congress is considering how to keep construction moving without making households subsidize it. Hawley is asking lawmakers to stop new construction until consumer protections are in place.

His position is not entirely new. In February, Hawley introduced the GRID Act with Democratic Senator Richard Blumenthal, seeking to make new data centers obtain electricity from their own sources rather than relying on existing grid supplies.

He now joins independent Senator Bernie Sanders and Democratic Representative Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez in publicly supporting a national pause. Their moratorium proposal goes further, calling for rules addressing AI's effects on workers and public safety as well as the environment.

The politics have shifted quickly. In the November 3 elections, voters backed restrictions on data center development across the country, including local bans and measures requiring public approval before construction.

A Pew Research Center analysis published Wednesday found 60% of Americans uncomfortable with a new data center in their area, including 54% of Republicans and 68% of Democrats. Another survey found 58% disliked political leaders who support building data centers near them.

Opponents worry about rising bills, water consumption and noise. Cities have already imposed restrictions, while OpenAI recently promised to cover electricity and grid costs at future campuses it owns and operates.

Industry supporters see a bigger economic risk. BlackRock CEO Larry Fink warned last month that a construction moratorium could trigger a recession because so much investment now depends on AI infrastructure.

A federal construction freeze could delay the additional computing capacity that AI companies expect to bring online, along with the power infrastructure built to support it. Existing data centers would not necessarily shut down, but the scope of Hawley's proposed moratorium remains undefined.

Hawley's call does not freeze a single project, and he has not said whether he would support the Sanders bill. For now, the Senate is debating legislation to speed infrastructure approvals, not legislation to ban new data centers.

‘Ebenezer’ Reviews Roundup: Johnny Depp’s Comeback Gets a Chilly Reception

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Critics are very split on Ti West's 'A Christmas Carol' adaptation, calling Depp everything from a "soulless worst Scrooge ever" to "eminently redeemable."

Nearly every critic reviewing Ebenezer seems to be finding their way around the same question: How do we feel, exactly, about Johnny Depp starring in a major studio film once again?

Conveniently, Ti West‘s adaptation of A Christmas Carol provides plenty of metaphorical fodder given it’s a redemption tale, after all, with Depp’s Scrooge given lines like, “I am not the man I was!” and “I was once good, and I know I can be again!”

Still, many critics aren’t sold on the film — or Depp, for that matter, with the actor once again buried in makeup and prosthetics. The film currently has a 51 percent positive score on Rotten Tomatoes, and even the positive reviews tend to be a bit tempered (it will be interesting to see if the audience score for Ebenezer is significantly higher, as critics assigned to cover the film might very well feel more negatively towards Depp than fans). The movie’s supporting cast — including Rupert Grint, Ian McKellen, Andrea Riseborough, Daisy Ridley and Tramell Tillman — are largely drawing praise. Related Stories Movies 'Ebenezer' Review: Johnny Depp Gets a Creepily Effective Comeback Vehicle in Ti West's Horror-Tinged Take on Dickens' Yuletide Classic Movies Johnny Depp's 'Ebenezer': First Reactions

Here is a sampling of reviews from major publications. Ebenezer opens Friday (watch the final trailer below).

The Hollywood Reporter: “Johnny Depp really needed a comeback vehicle, and he’s found an effective one in Ti West’s horror-tinged adaptation that allows the actor to deliver the sort of quirky theatrical performance for which he became famous. While Ebenezer doesn’t break any new ground, other than possibly being the first version of the story not really suitable for young children, it fulfills its ostensible goal of restoring the actor to A-list status … Depp … delivers a performance that no one would call subtle. In style, it most closely resembles a heavily made-up Albert Finney’s broad take on the character in the now largely forgotten 1970 musical Scrooge. But the actor’s charisma and star power eventually come through.”

Vulture: “Johnny Depp is the worst part of Ebenezer, but he’s also the reason it exists, so it’s futile to try and separate his presence from the rest of the movie, which is, inconveniently, pretty good. Depp is also doing a tiresome variation on the mugging that’s become his primary approach to acting ever since the role of Captain Jack Sparrow vaulted him into Disney-franchise levels of fame — a vaguely comic, scenery-chewing mode that doesn’t fit in with the rest of the movie…West’s interpretations of the spirits who visit the miser over the small hours of the night are imaginatively spooky, especially the Ghost of Christmas Past, played by Andrea Riseborough as a kind of corpse bride clad all in white with rheumy eyes.”

The New York Times: “It’s an attempt to, as much as one can in one role, play the hits. Whether consciously or simply out of a lack of creativity, the aging star is playing an agreeable version of himself, more than anything, aimed at providing a fond reminder of the Depp-in-drag iconography he crafted for himself over the last few decades … Ebenezer implicitly markets itself on the empirically shaky idea of a beloved Depp that was taken away. If you don’t squint too hard, his Scrooge might not only function as a return, but a memory wipe.”

The Times UK: “It’s just a shame, then, that the film around him, directed by the horror film-maker Ti West (Pearl, X), is hugely uninspired and has little to offer the already swollen canon of Christmas Carol adaptations. The Alastair Sim version from 1951, the Bill Murray comedy from 1988 and the Muppet musical reboot from 1992 are all vastly superior to this surprisingly vanilla take. … But really this is just a chance for audiences to see that Depp, like the character he is playing, is eminently redeemable and ready again, in the strongest possible terms, for his close-up.

Boston Globe: “Under all that makeup, Depp is easily the worst Scrooge I’ve ever seen. His performance is a soulless, hollow shell, never once convincing us of Scrooge’s miserly rage or his ultimate redemption. There isn’t an ounce of humanity in this performance; it may as well have been generated with AI.”

The Guardian: “As Scrooge himself, Depp is in fact rather good; theatrically bad-tempered and petulant and, as with his Jack Sparrow, I suspect Depp’s comedy timing and sonorous British accent might have been learned from his old Fast Show comrade Paul Whitehouse. He is better at the unredeemed Scrooge than the saved version at the end, where some of the abject pathos is missing. This is a decent enough new version, avoiding any big new revisionist takes which might have clunked.”

Slashfilm: “With the exception of a small moment here and there, Depp’s Scrooge is largely solid yet unremarkable. Which begs the question: why bother in the first place? It seems like the entirety of Ebenezer was arranged to allow Depp to chew scenery and showboat around, which the actor perversely refuses to do. It’s possible that Depp now considers such an approach something he’s no longer interested in, and it’s not like he hasn’t given grounded performances before, like his turn as John Dillinger in Public Enemies, for example. Depp’s performance as Scrooge simply doesn’t feel fully realized.”

IndieWire: “While Depp has never been the subtlest of actors, his post-Sparrow pivot away from artistic curiosity has distended his talents into such a grotesque caricature of their former charm that Ebenezer left me feeling like I may never be able to empathize with his screen presence again … Given the emphasis the director puts on the film’s genre spin, it’s ironic and surprising that Ebenezer is at its best when it focuses on the most basic human elements of its story, if only because the supporting characters offer such a welcome contrast to whatever Depp is doing.

The Daily Beast: “Ebenezer has enough commendable qualities to justify its existence, and Depp sturdily balances Scrooge’s nastiness, silliness, and, finally, kindness … Not fresh enough to become a classic, but with enough scariness to unnerve its younger viewers, West’s latest is a competent, accurate retelling fit for the Yuletide season—and, perhaps just as crucially, a Depp vehicle that suggests the beleaguered star isn’t ready to relinquish his A-list status just yet.”

Microsoft Goes Down, and the Media Business Takes Off Early

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A global outage of Microsoft systems has the entertainment business (and frankly all of corporate America) reeling, and employees cutting out early.

It’s not just you: The Runyon Canyon and Baldwin Hills trails are unusually busy. The New Jersey Transit trains to Montclair and Metro North trains to Westchester really are more crowded.

The media business took off early on Thursday, and the bosses can blame Microsoft.

The tech giant suffered a stunning global outage Thursday, putting Outlook, Teams, CoPilot and online version of Word, Excel and other software on ice, and work grinding (mostly) to a halt.

Inside one midtown Manhattan media office, one employee said that as soon as it became clear the services weren’t coming back soon, folks headed for the door, braving the rain to try and get home early. Inside one newsroom, the programming and newsgathering had to go on, but one staffer said that the conversations migrated to Slack and text chains.

The media business may still rely on old-fashioned technology: Film reels, cable wires and broadcast spectrum, but the internet has become its backbone, and an outage of this scale can grind an industry (or in this case much of the economy) to a halt.

But as eerie as the Microsoft-induced quiet is, it is what comes next that surely terrifies the media professionals: Any hopes of reaching inbox zero will be complicated by the inbox 1,000 waiting once the tech servers are back online.

Still, at least folks can get that early hike in before the sunset, or catch an earlier train (maybe even off-peak!) home.

The media business can get itself back in order Friday. If Microsoft lets it.

Now That We Finally Know Adults Season 3 Is Happening, I Need To See 3 Things

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Nobody Wants This Cast Proposed A New Lead For Season 4 (I Need This To Happen)

I’m Excited For Two More Seasons Of Heated Rivalry, But I Have One Concern

Mistletoe Murders Is Back For Season 3 With A Santa-Based Mystery And Some Big Hallmark Guest Stars

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Gen-Z comedy, Adults, dropped its sophomore season on FX and Hulu as part of the 2026 TV Schedule back in August, and since then fans have been loudly campaigning for another season. After months of waiting, the series was officially renewed for a Season 3, much to the delight of the fans.

The cast took to Instagram to share the news with their devoted viewers, which included the reveal that the third season will include 16 original episodes, nearly double the length of the first two seasons. As a massive fan of the show and a firm believer that sitcoms need more episodes, I’m thrilled at the news! In fact, I’m so excited I’ve already compiled a list of things I absolutely need the extended season to feature.

Holiday episodes used to be a sitcom staple, but as television seasons started getting shorter and shorter, these episodes started to disappear. I had accepted the fact that I would never get to see my favorite characters celebrate birthdays or the holiday season, but now that might not be the case.

With a 16-episode season comes space for these beloved one-off episodes. Just think of all the hijinks that could ensue from the Adults gang trying to celebrate the holidays. We’ve already seen them struggle to host a sophisticated dinner party in the first season. Now imagine it’s the holiday season and Paul Baker’s family decides to make the trek to New York to spend Christmas with their son and his new husband after his sister lets it slip that he’s married. The shenanigans would be hilarious!

Over the course of the first two seasons, Adults has found time to include some fun guest stars like actor Charlie Cox, actress/model Julia Fox, and sitcom royalty Raven-Symoné. I’m sure the tradition will continue in Season 3, and I have some ideas on whom the creators should reach out to.

Since Adults films in Canada, it makes total sense that the Gen-Z show should approach a breakout Gen-Z actor who also appears on a hit Canadian-produced show. Yes, I’m talking about Heated Rivalry’s Hudson Williams. Williams could play himself and act as an old friend from Paul Baker’s childhood, since he canonically grew up in Canada and has a history of befriending famous people without realizing it.

Another obvious, albeit more difficult to get, choice would be New York’s Mayor Zohran Mamdani. I can totally see Issa finding a way to reach out to the Mayor about some minor inconvenience affecting her city that no one else cares about.

Season 2 ended on a major high note with Paul Baker and Anton finally getting together, but it also shocked fans with a plot twist in the final moments that featured Billie and Samir hooking up. Don’t get me wrong, I can’t wait to see Paul Baker and Anton enter their “relationship era,” but I’m also excited to see what shenanigans Billie and Samir get into now that they’ve crossed such a huge line in their friendship.

On one hand, I’d love Billie and Samir to channel the relationship between Chandler and Monica from Friends and try to keep their hookup and subsequent relationship a secret from the rest of the house. It would be difficult considering how close they all are, but definitely worth it to keep it away from everyone meddling.

However, I could also see them trying to act as if their hookup was casual and something they can continue doing without catching feelings. Neither Billie nor Samir do anything without their whole heart involved, so this would be a recipe for disaster but also great for laughs.

Season 3 has so much room for fun and hijinks with its longer episode order, and I can’t wait to see what the writers come up with. While it’ll be a while before the new season is out, fans can stream the first two seasons with a Hulu subscription now.

Aspyn Ovard on Her Divorce, Being in a Throuple and Joining ‘The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives’ Universe: ‘I Hope People Can Understand Me a Little Bit Better’

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SPOILER ALERT: This story contains major spoilers for “The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives,” now streaming on Hulu.

Not even 15 years of living life online could have prepared Aspyn Ovard for the release of “The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives: Orange County.”

The show, a spinoff of Hulu’s uber-popular, ever-controversial Utah-based franchise, hit small screens everywhere on Nov. 12, in which Ovard stars alongside other cast members including Bobbi Althoff, Avery Woods, Salomé Andrea, McCall DaPron and Jordan McClanahan. But a week before its release, despite all those years and millions of eyeballs watching her every move (first on YouTube, where she was then known by the username HauteBrilliance, then on Instagram and now on TikTok), the 30-year-old was feeling anxious about the unknowns that come with putting everything out there on reality TV. Related Stories 'PUBG' Live-Action Series From Donald Glover, Stephen Glover in Development at Netflix Netflix Grapples With Digital-Ad Squeeze Amid Widening Glut of Streaming Commercials

There are the obvious considerations: How will those fans — and a new audience — react to her life? “It’s so different than social media, so I really have like no idea what to expect,” she tells Variety over Zoom from a West Hollywood hotel room. Given that she’s spent the better half of her life as an influencer, major milestones have been well-marked; among other things, fans have watched the Utah-born creator meet, date and marry — all by 19 — Parker Ferris, and welcome three daughters (Cove, Lola and Elle), by 27. In 2024, right after her youngest was born, Ovard filed for divorce, a move that shocked those who’d come of age watching the high-school sweethearts.

Then, newly single for the first time in her adult life, Ovard met Bri Davis. Fast forward two years, and she’s now in a relationship with both Davis and Davis’ husband, Billy — a throuple, as they call themselves. In the show’s first episode, we meet said throuple, all of whom appear really, truly, deeply in love, despite Ferris’ disapproval. (“You guys are clout chasing!” Ferris tells Billy and Bri in a tense early scene. “It’s so obvious.”)

And yet, despite Ferris’ objection to the whole situation, he remains committed to co-parenting with Ovard, which brings them to a heated couples therapy session in which audiences learn something they’ve kept completely offline: Their daughter has terminal cancer. “Everything happened so out of nowhere,” Ovard explains in the episode, never naming which daughter is sick. “One day she was OK, and the next day she wasn’t.”

Given the sensitive nature of the situation, reps for Hulu asked that Ovard not be asked any questions — tangential or direct — about her children and their health. But even so, it’s clear that revealing what Ovard has been privately dealing with for the past three years is what’s causing her nervousness ahead of the show’s release, as much as she appreciates how carefully production has handled her story, she says.

“The internet has kind of been my last piece of normalcy for my life, so saying goodbye to that is definitely very life-changing for me. I don’t really know how I’m gonna navigate it,” she says. “I literally live day by day, minute by minute a lot of the time, so I’ll just be figuring it out as I go. I’ve already been through so much, so I know I’ll survive.”

Below, Ovard spoke with Variety about sharing her life, her connection to Mormonism, her current status with Ferris and that fight with McCall and Jordan.

I’m trying to remember if I was ever approached for the Utah show when I lived there. I think this is probably the first time, especially for something in California, that I’ve been approached for. I probably said in a Q&A years ago that I would never do reality TV, but, obviously, you never know where life’s going to take you. I’m such a big fan of the Utah show, and literally have been since day one. Like, I love those girls and have seen them be able to do such incredible things with such an incredible business opportunity. Especially as the breadwinner and provider for my family, it was an obvious yes — to do something new career-wise, and then be able to build up these friendships even more and have a better sense of community in Orange County. It felt like the perfect next step for me.

Technically, I am baptized into the Mormon Church. I don’t identify with being Mormon, but I’ve been to the Mormon Church a million times. There’s so much Mormon influence in my life, even just with getting married at 19 and having kids young — the culture has shaped me so much, whether I like that or not.

I think there’s so much [the cast] can gain from each other. I mean, everyone is so different, and especially the girls who are active Mormons, there’s so much that they can learn from us girls who are very progressive and very liberal, and have been able to have so much life experience and been able to see the world thanks to our careers on social media. I’m really excited for those girls to also be able to join in on that and be able to provide for their families, and also just get a bigger worldview from everything that we’re able to do. But even though I’m not a Mormon wife, I feel like I definitely have a place on the show, and I think it makes sense that I’m there.

When I was initially approached about the show, I didn’t want to share my relationship. I wasn’t publicly posting about anything. We’d only been together for a year, and they had just moved [to OC]. But again, it’s just such an incredible opportunity, so we talked about it a lot. I could have done the show alone, but I think that there’s just been so many pieces of my life that I haven’t shared for the last two years that it kind of was just — there was gonna be a point where had to put everything out there in a way, and I didn’t know what that would look like for me, or how I would handle that, or just really anything about it. But we talked about it a lot, and did obviously end up deciding to just go all in. We’re so good at just disconnecting: like, that’s work, and then we have like our real life and our relationship. And as much as we were so open with everything, it is nice to be in a very stable relationship where we could handle something like this and feel confident about going into this insane opportunity.

Yeah, I feel like not a lot of husbands were really on it. There was definitely stuff with Billy that we filmed. I mean, everyone filmed stuff, and it gets cut. But Billy also does work nine-to-five, so he didn’t have as much flexibility with filming, but it also made sense for Bri to be at more things than him, like the girls’ trips. The girls love Bri and Billy, but it makes sense to bring Bri around a little more often than Billy.

Obviously, it’s a lot to go through a divorce, and then to have your ex have a new relationship, and not only that, but a different type of relationship, and to have people around your kids. Our life just is so sensitive. It makes sense that he’s very protective of our girls and even of me.

But luckily, we are in a much better spot. I think he just needed time. We’re literally going on vacation together next week, when the show comes out. I want us all to be able to get away: me, Billy, Bri, Parker, the girls. It’s nice to be in a place where we can handle something like that now.

I think it would have happened without the show. I really think that the answer was just time. But there are opportunities that come up from the show, because we’re like, “OK, we need to have these conversations,” and do things together, like the beach dinner and couples therapy. All of that stuff is a part of the show, but it’s also just part of our real lives. But I think with time and just seeing the consistency and stability that they’ve been able to provide for me, and also for our kids, he’s come around more.

I was able to see a few things as they were editing — anything that was very sensitive, they let me have a hand in helping craft it and to make sure I was comfortable. Production was very, very good to me with my story and just the sensitivity of everything that I have going on. So as hard as it is to watch those scenes back, I’ve watched them a handful of times now and have seen a couple different versions of everything. But it definitely is hard to watch that back. It’s just a lot. But I’m grateful that we’re in a little bit of a better place now than we were back then.

I think people will be underwhelmed by it. People, especially on TikTok, have really, really crazy theories. But I think that when you get to see the full context of my life and what I’ve been through the last few years, it makes sense why this was so painful to me. When I learned all of that, it was two months after my dad died from alcoholism, so it was very earth-shattering to me to learn about those things that went on and I wasn’t aware. And especially friends knowing, and his family. It just completely changes the way you look at your life and what went on in your marriage. It was something very sensitive to me, and I think people would have been disappointed if it wasn’t included. But Parker himself was willing to talk about it, and with me sharing a lot of the traumatic things I’ve been through in the last couple of years, that was a big piece of it.

I think Jen is so brave. It’s really difficult to make the decision to get divorced, and especially when pregnant. I mean, I technically filed right after I had my baby, but I was going through all of it while pregnant. I just feel for Jen so much, and relate to her so much. I remember how lonely it was going through my divorce, and I was going through so much else at the time too. It was just like unbearable agony. And so, if I can help Jen out in any way by being there for her, I’m so happy to do that and so honored to get to be a support person to her.

I’m definitely in a good place with everyone, but I would say I am probably the closest with Avery. We’ve been able to develop such a close friendship — we’ve been friends for years, but especially throughout filming, when you’re getting to actually see each other and hang out more, it definitely brought us a lot closer. She’s been such a good support person for me, and I’m so grateful for that.

I know! I’ve told her that the whole time. Like, she’s such a good friend. She comes across so well. She’s so funny. She’s such an important piece of the show, and the friend group as a whole. She’s such a good mediator.

It was bigger than just whether they support me in my relationship. It’s gay rights as a whole, and the Mormon Church has a very clear stance that they do not recognize or perform same-sex marriages — you can check their website. They are not supportive of the LGBTQIA+ community, so it’s very interesting to have to navigate having devout Mormon friends who are saying, “No, but I support you, and I support your relationship.” I’m very black and white.

McCall and Jordan have been supportive of me, but to me it was a bigger issue. They also cut around politics in the show. Like, it was a much bigger conversation. So the only thing hard to watch back is how much of that gets cut out, because I look a little crazy and I’m a little heated, but that is just a hill I will die on. I’m very fiery, and so I’m just like, “I will fight you on this.”

They were trying to say that they support me, so I was like, “OK, so say that you support gay rights.” The conversation was cut down quite a bit. So it looks like they say it pretty quickly, but I mean — I’m very clear with my stance on the church, and them wanting me to say something that I didn’t believe in felt like two opposite things. I was trying to get them to say something they say they do believe in, whereas they wanted me to say something that is not true. So those did feel like different things to me.

When you know the full picture of my life, it’s easier to understand a lot of the decisions that I make, even though other friends also have known the full picture and not decided to stick around and be a part of my village. I do feel lucky that everyone was, you know, very accepting. I mean, anytime I show up anywhere and I don’t have Bri with me, they’re like, “Where is she? Why didn’t you bring her?” Or if it’s a big group thing, Billy. Everybody just loves them and has been willing to get to know them, and I think that’s something I didn’t really experience with other people who are not in my life anymore. I’m just grateful that they were open-minded to that different type of relationship, especially the more conservative girls, because I know that that is something that they’ve like really never been around. So all I can ask is that they’re open-minded, and they have been, and they’ve been great.

I definitely feel very nervous. As much as I really appreciate the way that production handled my story, and I know that they were so careful in crafting it, I think it’s obvious why I would be very anxious for the show to come out, and I think that the internet has just kind of been like my last piece of normalcy in my life, so saying goodbye to that is definitely very life-changing for me. I don’t really know how I’m gonna navigate it, but I’ll definitely be taking some time off to just try and figure that out. And again, I literally live day by day, minute by minute, a lot of the time, so I’ll just be figuring it out as I go. I’ve already been through so much, so I know I’ll survive and be fine. But it is definitely an anxiety-inducing event for me for this to come out. It’s so different than social media, so I really have like no idea what to expect.

I hope people can understand me a little bit better. It’s a really interesting feeling because I’ve never really cared about that, and as much as I’ve shared, I’ve been very careful with what I’ve shared, and I’ve always had the amount of privacy that I wanted. Really, for the past, like, 16 years. It’s a very new feeling for me to hope that people understand me. But I think in sharing so much, I just hope that the world can see a more full picture of my life and understand me a little bit better from the last few years.

This interview has been edited and condensed.

Was This New Fable Gameplay Meant to Sell the Game?

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I am pretty confident Fable will be a fantastic RPG when it finally releases in February 2027, but for those still sat on the fence, Xbox is going to have to do a better job of promoting it than this latest example.

To mark the 25th anniversary of Xbox, four minutes of gameplay taken from a quest has been uploaded. However, all of maybe 30 seconds of it actually has anything going on. Watch it above.

Obviously, you do indeed do a lot of walking during open world RPGs, but it's not something you usually feature much in marketing beats. That's what most of the latest gameplay slice for Fable is, though: walking.

Partway through, there is a combat sequence showing off swordfighting, archery, and magical abilities. It's over quickly, however, and then we're back to walking. The video then concludes with a cutscene lacking context as it's been plucked out of the full game to be seen online.

I grew up playing the three mainline Fable games on the Original Xbox and Xbox 360; I have very fond memories of this series, so I want the new rebooted instalment to do well. It'll need better presentations and trailers than this for a good chance at that. It's out for PS5, Xbox Series X|S, and PC on 23rd February 2027.

NK hackers join memecoin trenches, xStocks adds Hong Kong equities: Asia Express

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

North Korean hackers launch memecoins to launder funds stolen in the Binance hack, while xStocks plans to add hundreds of Hong Kong-listed equities in the coming weeks.

North Korean hackers created memecoins to launder cryptocurrency stolen in the $6.3 billion Binance hack, according to a report from blockchain analytics firm Elliptic.Between Nov. 5 and 7, the hackers created at least six tokens on NEAR and used liquidity pools on decentralized exchanges to move $430,000 of stolen funds between wallets, Elliptic said. However, the tokens attracted almost no outside trading and were abandoned shortly after launch.

Elliptic said the same laundering technique was also used following the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in 2025, when North Korean-linked actors moved at least $24 million through a newly created token on the TRON blockchain.

Hong Kong’s wealthy investors are embracing crypto. (Chi Hung Wong)

Kraken parent Payward’s xStocks platform has added tokenized versions of more than 200 stocks listed in the United Kingdom and European Union.

Payward said the latest expansion brings xStocks’ offering to more than 1,500 tokenized assets. The company reported more than $45 billion in cumulative transaction volume and over 550,000 unique holders.

Payward said it plans to add hundreds of Hong Kong-listed equities in the coming weeks.

Deputy Secretary for Financial Services Chan Ho-lim told FinTech Week the region will introduces a new licensing regime for virtual-asset trading, custody, advisory and management, via amendment bills that will be submitted to Legislative Council this year.

Police have arrested 22 people in a cross border money laundering investigation, that allegedly involved a over the counter crypto network that moved more than HKD$150 million (US$18 million) while attempting to obscure the origin of funds. Meanwhile, the US has sanctioned 41 entities allegedly linked to the Cambodia’s Prince Group, including a man named Qiu Yong, who is accused of running Hong Kong companies on behalf of the criminal organization.

South Korea’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) plans to allow crypto exchanges to suspend deposits and withdrawals when hacking is suspected, rather than waiting until a breach is confirmed.

The FSC said it would issue a no-action letter permitting preventive suspensions under certain conditions. Current rules prohibit exchanges from blocking withdrawals without legitimate grounds and explicitly allow restrictions only when a hacking incident is clearly established.

The regulator also called for round-the-clock monitoring and incident response, higher cold-wallet holdings, and stronger insurance coverage and reserves.

South Korean crypto exchange GOPAX announced on November 6 it was suspending trading, deposits and withdrawals and moving all user assets to cold wallets. The firm had received threatening emails that claimed a hack was imminent, and took the drastic step of putting everything on ice on the recommendation of financial authorities.

Japanese financial services giant Nomura Holdings is reportedly weighing a sale of its cryptocurrency subsidiary Laser Digital as Nomura’s European operations continue to post losses.

Nomura has held talks with potential buyers, including Marex Group but is yet to make a final decision and could keep the business.

Nomura’s European businesses posted a pretax loss of 7.4 billion yen ($46.5 million) in the quarter ended Sept. 30, 2026, their fourth consecutive quarterly loss. The company posted pretax profits in every other reporting region over the same period.

Thailand is examining new rules for ETFs and ICO investors (Sasint)

Thailand’s securities regulator has proposed capping daily stablecoin deposits and withdrawals at 5 million baht ($148,000) each per customer at regulated crypto operators.

The limit would apply separately to deposits and withdrawals at each operator, Thailand’s Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) said in a Thursday announcement. The regulator said the rules aim to curb money laundering, cybercrime and attempts to bypass cross-border money transfer rules.

The draft would bar stablecoin transfers to and from third-party wallets.

Thailand’s SEC will block access to Bitget from December 5. It has also proposed new tests for retail investors before allowing them to invest in ICOs. The tests would require knowledge of at least six areas including digital tokens and investor rights, blockchain technology, digital-asset wallets and distribution channels. The proposals will be up for comment until November 25. The SEC is also seeking feedback on new rules around margin loans and ETFs, but has ruled out allowing leveraged or crypto ETFs at this stage.

South Korean crypto firm Bithumb Asset, has invested $85 million into a luxury resort project in Hoi An in Vietnam being built by local real estate developer Asia Estate. Da Nang police have issued a warning about scams via “ghost” cryptocurrency projects that are really just ponzi schemes in disguise. They highlighted a fraud ring involving the DRK cryptocurrency project, led by Huynh Duc Van, 33, and seven accomplices, which was recently dealt with by law enforcement.

Nomura’s former head of crypto derivatives trading, John Kim, has joined Chicago-based DV Trading in Singapore to lead a new discretionary crypto-trading team. A Singapore woman lost S$78,000 (US$60,391) in a four-month long crypto scam after someone posing as an insurance buyer “helped” her to set up a crypto wallet. She initially believed she was making money, before getting cleared out after she was directed to the little known Qevorinex platform. International bank communication network Swift argued at Singapore FinTech Festival for shared standards to prevent digital assets from fragmenting across incompatible systems.

Silicon Valley Is Still Defending Jensen Huang

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

News of Jensen Huang’s affair with an alleged asset of China’s Ministry of State Security was first met with a muted response in Silicon Valley—including inside Nvidia itself. Huang’s resignation, after news of two other alleged romantic relationships with Nvidia staffers, has left both the company and the broader AI industry stunned.

As revelations about Huang’s initial affair came out earlier this week, rank-and-file Nvidia employees at first pursued business as usual, according to two current and three former employees at the chipmaker, speaking anonymously to protect relationships at the company. They trusted the board to investigate and act accordingly, but few workers thought Huang would actually leave.

Nvidia’s roughly 60,000 employees are loyal to the point that its global turnover rate is reportedly among the lowest in tech. Workers say they have heard it called having “green in your blood” or being “green-pilled” or on “team green,” referring to the company’s signature color. Huang was the company’s soul and the founder who stuck with it through challenges for 33 years. Employees appreciated that he kept layoffs rare and red tape sparing.

“In just three days we went from superhero cape to ‘Oops, I forgot my pants,’ and nobody’s quite sure how to swallow that,” one employee said. One former employee insisted in Huang’s defense that something “more sinister” must have been in play, and that affairs alone shouldn’t have led to his departure.

Huang’s peers in Silicon Valley echoed that sentiment, remaining vocal in their support of the embattled CEO even after his ouster. “One mistake does not erase a lifetime of leadership and contribution in building NVDA,” venture capitalist Antonio Gracias wrote on X earlier this week, using the company’s ticker symbol.

The New York Times reported on Wednesday that Huang has also had an extramarital affair with a vice president at Nvidia, and Reuters revealed on Thursday a second “intimate relationship” with an employee. Before those alleged affairs became public, multiple industry executives told WIRED that Huang is “irreplaceable” and that his biggest mistake, aside from his relationship with Alice Huang, was admitting the affair in writing to the whole company.

That appears to still be the prevailing sentiment. “A founder who has spent 30 years building such a unique company so capably has earned the presumption that he should keep leading it,” venture capitalist Sarah Guo wrote on X after Huang resigned, and a few hours before the Reuters report.

Authorities and Nvidia’s board have yet to formally accuse Huang of wrongdoing in his interactions with the businesswoman and suspected intelligence asset Alice Huang, and China has denied that she’s a spy. President Donald Trump said on Tuesday that the FBI “is looking into it.” Nvidia’s board has stated that it doesn’t believe Huang compromised any secrets, but it has hired the law firm Wachtell Lipton to investigate.

Add to all that, some corners of Nvidia had already been whispering about the relationship with Alice Huang; one venture capitalist who declined to be named recalled a recent dinner where an Nvidia executive claimed there had been rumors about Jensen’s romantic relationship with a young Asian woman.

On Monday, the venture capitalist had estimated a “less than 10 percent” chance Huang would lose his job at the top of the world’s most valuable company. “The wind’s going to blow over on this,” they said. “People are going to hate him for three months, and forget this ever happened.”

The big anxiety in Silicon Valley remains the possibility that instability at Nvidia will burst an AI bubble, but analysts aren’t so sure.

The chief executive of one AI company, who spoke on condition of anonymity so as not to affect business relationships, says Nvidia’s recent bankrolling of AI startups and even buying companies such as model-hosting provider Hugging Face has helped prop up the need for chips. A “new professional CEO won’t do any of that,” meaning “systemic risk for the AI trade and AI bubble increases substantially,” the entrepreneur says.

Between bubble talk and Huang rumors, Nvidia shares in recent days have sunk to their lowest point in more than a year. Nvidia reports its latest quarterly earnings next week.

Inside Nvidia, meetings, projects, and the company’s aggressive culture are rapidly pressing ahead with what’s left of Huang’s vision.

Additional reporting by Will Knight and Zoë Schiffer.

FAA expands AI advice to manage air traffic congestion across entire US airspace

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Airlines praised the AI tool, but the air traffic controllers’ union wasn’t involved.

The US government has expanded the use of an AI tool for advising air traffic controllers to assist in managing air traffic across the entire national airspace system. Airline executives have voiced support for the rollout, but the US labor union representing air traffic controllers has said it was not involved in designing or testing the AI-driven system.

The Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) says the $875 million SMART system has helped reroute 180 flights since being implemented in airspace over Washington, DC, on September 21. The agency claims that this saved an average of 10 minutes per flight and almost 80 gallons of fuel per flight—and it is apparently hoping such results can be replicated nationwide through the expanded deployment.

“After a successful initial rollout to the National Capital Region in September, the FAA will now use SMART in the airspace of all 22 centers across the country to reduce delays, reduce fuel consumption and improve the travel experience for the flying public,” according to the agency statement posted on November 10.

The FAA fact sheet describes SMART as a cloud-based system that combines information about “weather patterns, flight paths, traffic flow and controller staffing metrics” to predict air traffic patterns. It is designed to make scheduling recommendations, identify optimal flight routes, and determine the best order of arrivals for aircraft.

However, a human team of FAA specialists based in DC is ultimately responsible for validating the SMART system recommendations before passing them along to air traffic control facilities across the country, according to the agency.

Furthermore, the AI-powered SMART system “cannot be used to replace the critical role air traffic controllers play or take over control of an aircraft,” the FAA clarified in the September 21 announcement about the initial deployment. Local air traffic controllers can decide whether or not to accept the SMART system recommendations.

The previous FAA announcement included supportive statements from Delta Air Lines CEO Ed Bastian and American Airlines CEO Robert Isom.

But the National Air Traffic Controllers Association, which represents more than 15,000 members, also told Flying magazine that it was not consulted or involved in the “design, testing, or implementation of SMART and therefore cannot speak to its effect on the air traffic control workforce.”

Following the initial deployment in airspace above DC, the FAA expanded the SMART system’s coverage to air route traffic control centers in Atlanta along with Jacksonville and Miami in Florida, according to The Dallas Morning News.

The FAA also shared an example with The Dallas Morning News about how SMART recommended a shorter and faster route for a cargo flight arriving at Dallas-Fort Worth International Airport, although the newspaper noted that the agency did not identify the flight involved and has not provided information about how SMART may have assisted passenger flight traffic.

Ars has previously covered the SMART system’s development and lingering questions about how it works, as well as the air traffic controller shortage and other challenges facing the FAA.

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Muse can now place calls to U.S. businesses — if you pay

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Meta’s Muse, which now has over 10 million users, the company said last month, has been working to make consumer AI a mainstream product for everyday users. Placing calls is one of the ways it aims to get there, as it could handle another task on people’s to-do lists.

Notes Alexandr Wang, Meta’s Chief AI officer and head of its Superintelligence Labs division, the feature could be used to take care of calls people don’t want to make, like those to their cable company, the DMV, or to a restaurant that doesn’t offer online reservations.

🚨MUSE FEATURE ALERT!!!🚨lots of you have been asking for this one…muse can make phone calls for you! ☎️📞📲let muse take care of all those calls you don’t want to make. insurance? cable? DMV? dinner res? not your problem anymore.out now to paid subs in the US 🇺🇸 pic.twitter.com/dsyMNmI2Ub

Previously, only a select number of Muse early adopters were able to try this feature after asking the AI for access. Now, anyone who pays for Muse can make this type of request. Currently, Muse subscription plans start at $20 per month for 500 million Muse tokens per week. Heavier AI users can opt to pay $100 per month for 3 billion tokens.

Muse is not the only AI agent able to place calls to businesses; the startup Instinct has been offering a similar option, as has Google’s Gemini. However, while the AI tools are now certainly capable of handling the task, the reception on the other end of the line can be mixed. Many businesses and receptionists hang up on calls from AI agents as soon as they identify that it’s not a human on the phone. But with AI now better able to mimic a real human voice, being able to tell the difference between a customer and an AI working on the customer’s behalf will get tougher.

Despite the challenges, this feature is one that could appeal to consumers who have grown frustrated with the state of customer service over the years. For too long, customers have been forced to navigate complex phone trees, and then sit on hold waiting for a human representative to pick up. If they’re lucky, that person can actually help them, and not transfer them to another department. Now, those consumers could potentially use their AI, with its infinite patience, for this tedious task instead.

While calls remain a paid-only feature for the time being, the recently added option to make a Muse email address will soon roll out to free users, Wang said this week.

AI Is Dividing Product Designers: Exploring vs. Building

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Design Engineer at Matter Labs (creators of ZKsync and Prividium). Creator of DecentWealth, ScrollChess, and a few other apps.

Design Engineer at Matter Labs (creators of ZKsync and Prividium). Creator of DecentWealth, ScrollChess, and a few other apps.

Design Engineer at Matter Labs (creators of ZKsync and Prividium). Creator of DecentWealth, ScrollChess, and a few other apps.

A week ago, I attended PUSH UX in Munich, Europe's largest conference on product design and user experience, featuring speakers from Anthropic, Wise, Google, IKEA, n8n, and other prominent organizations. On my way home, I kept thinking about one recurring theme: product design teams are approaching AI from fundamentally different perspectives.

Some are cautiously experimenting with AI while trying to fit it into established processes. Others have already integrated it into their daily workflows, connecting research, prototyping, design, and code with fewer handoffs. The difference goes beyond tool adoption. It reflects how teams understand their work, organize collaboration, and measure product value.

Paulo Oliveira from IKEA highlighted a fundamental problem with the way organizations approach AI:

"Most efforts start with tools, copilots, and automation, and mistake faster cycles for value. The result is a pile of pilots nobody adopted, and a lot of people wondering what happened to the work they were good at. The alternative: to build something that fits, lasts, and makes an organization better at what it already does well. That starts by refusing the AI first, AI everything instinct."

I think this captures one of the central challenges in product design today.

Adding AI to an existing workflow can make individual tasks faster without improving the product or the way a team operates. Organizations can accumulate tools and experiments while leaving the underlying processes unchanged.

Meaningful adoption starts with understanding where teams lose time, which handoffs create friction, and where AI can improve how products are researched, designed, built, and validated.

For years, product design and software development operated as distinct stages. Designers worked in Figma, documented interactions, prepared specifications, and handed their work to engineers for implementation. This division made sense when building and testing functional interfaces required substantial engineering resources. AI-assisted development is changing that relationship.

After years of designing fintech and blockchain-based products, I kept encountering the same limitation: I could design an experience without having full control over how it was implemented.

About a year ago, I started building websites and applications from scratch with AI tools. As I described in my previous HackerNoon article, Claude Code Finally Made Me an Engineer After 10 Years in Figma, my workflow shifted from almost entirely Figma to roughly 90% Claude Code.

Working closer to production code lets me build, test, and iterate on interfaces directly, with fewer handoffs between design and engineering.

For product teams, this opens up several possibilities:

This doesn't mean every designer needs to become a full-time software engineer. Deep specialization remains essential, particularly in complex products. However, designers can work closer to implementation and contribute beyond the traditional boundaries of their roles.

Another exciting development is how AI is changing prototyping. Think about how difficult it used to be to create an interactive prototype for a specific device, such as an Apple Watch or an in-car infotainment system. Even without writing production code, designers can now use AI to build interactive prototypes tailored to specific hardware and explore how their ideas work in real-world contexts.

As AI makes prototyping, coding, and exploring alternatives easier, the focus of design work is shifting toward decision-making and product quality. Understanding whether it solves the right problem, meets user needs, and works reliably requires experience and critical judgment.

This is where faster workflows can create lasting value. Teams have more opportunities to test assumptions, explore alternatives, and identify problems before committing significant resources to a solution. However, individual productivity gains aren't enough. Organizations need to connect design, engineering, and product teams around shared workflows, clear quality standards, and meaningful evaluation of AI-generated output.

I'm particularly interested in how this can shape an AI-first product culture. The goal is to encourage experimentation while maintaining the technical rigor, security, and reliability that deep tech products demand.

Many designers argue that AI-generated product experiences lack quality. Are they right? Sometimes. Can the results improve? Absolutely.

AI-assisted research and discovery can produce shallow or generic results, especially when designers rely on AI without applying their own expertise and critical judgment. The quality of the outcome depends on how thoughtfully people guide, evaluate, and refine the process. Julia Bastian from Alasco explored this challenge in a session on AI discovery slop, examining how to recognize and address low-quality AI-generated research and insights.

The PUSH UX conference reinforced how differently the design community is responding to AI. Some teams are still finding their footing, while others are already changing how they work. My own experience has made me more comfortable working across design, product strategy, and engineering, but every team will need to find its own approach.

The important shift is to reconsider established workflows, explore where new capabilities can make a difference, and take greater ownership of business outcomes.

25 OG Xbox titles are now on PC, and it's an utterly bizarre list of games

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

This weekend marks Xbox's 25th birthday, and while any celebration of the milestone is going to be muted in view of the layoff bloodbath Microsoft is currently engaging in, the gaming division is taking steps to keep us thinking about the brand in a positive light. That includes a fresh expansion to the list of original Xbox games that are now backwards compatible on PC.

After the four original Xbox games that Microsoft brought to PC earlier this year, another 21 have joined the list – a total of 25 games for the console's 25th birthday. The new list of games is an utterly bizarre one, largely dominated by LucasArts titles. That includes plenty of beloved Star Wars titles, alongside largely forgotten obscurities like Secret Weapons Over Normandy and Thrillville.

Some of the titles you might recognize, like Star Wars classics Battlefront 2, Jedi Outcast, and Knights of the Old Republic, are odd choices in themselves, since those games are already available on PC. Assuming Xbox is using the same emulation-based solution to port these games, they're all going to be substantially worse experiences than simply picking up the native PC versions for the same price on Steam.

That goes double for The Elder Scrolls 3: Morrowind, which is also on the list of newly added games. The Xbox version of Morrowind was a stunning technical achievement back in 2002, but it was notoriously janky even at the time, filled with bugs and technical limitations that have been robustly addressed on PC. I struggle to come up with a compelling reason anyone would want to play this version over the PC original today, especially with all the mods available for the RPG.

There are two games I absolutely will not complain about, however: TimeSplitters 2 and TimeSplitters: Future Perfect. Developed by the Rare veterans at Free Radical Design, the TimeSplitters series was a spiritual successor to classic N64 games GoldenEye and Perfect Dark, refreshing the FPS gameplay for a new generation with fun weapon gimmicks and charmingly goofy characters.

TimeSplitters never moved beyond cult classic status, and attempts to revive the series ended in failure. None of these games ever got PC ports, which makes their inclusion arguably the most notable part of this Xbox backwards compatibility update.

Gemini trading volume drops 73% as Q3 revenue falls to $41 million

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Gemini narrowed its quarterly loss to $58.6 million as costs fell, while prediction market revenue rose 52% from Q2.

Gemini's trading volume is down 73% from a year earlier in the third quarter, dragging revenue lower despite cost cuts narrowing its losses.

The exchange reported $40.9 million in total revenue on Thursday, down 19% from $50.6 million seen a year ago and 10% from the second quarter. Its net loss shrank to $58.6 million from nearly $160 million a year earlier and $107.7 million in Q2.

Shares of Gemini Space Station Inc. Gemini Space Station Inc. (GEMI) rose 6.5% in after-hours trading to $2.79 but remain down around 75% year to date.

Operating expenses fell 30% year over year to roughly $120 million.

"Operating expenses declined for a third consecutive quarter, and as we invest in growth, we intend to stay disciplined about where those dollars go," Gemini CEO Tyler Winklevoss said in a statement.

Exchange revenue also dropped 47% to $13.4 million, stemming from a trading volume decline to $4.5 billion from $16.4 billion last year at the height of the 2025 crypto market bull run. Still, volume grew 18% from the previous quarter.

Credit card revenue slipped 4% from the prior quarter, bringing in $15.5 million. Meanwhile, assets on the platform jumped to $13.4 billion from Q2's $8.4 billion.

Gemini's prediction market segment generated $800,000 in revenue, up 52% quarter over quarter. The company is looking to make its exchange and clearinghouse open to brokerages that want to offer event contracts.

In August, Gemini signed a letter of intent with Apex Fintech Solutions to offer crypto event contracts through Apex, though the deal is still not finalized.

"Gemini 2.0 is our bet that the platforms that last will be the ones that own the regulated infrastructure underneath," Gemini President Cameron Winklevoss said.

And I think if you're looking for stability and you're looking for someone who's not going to create new drama and who's not going to have a scandal, that seems like she really fits that bill.

Zoë Schiffer: Yeah, I think what they need right now is stability, like you said. They need someone to take the reins. They don't want anything else dramatic to happen to the company. And of course they're going to immediately kick off a search for a new visionary leader, which as we said in the last episode, there's not an obvious successor here. They've got to find someone who has incredible technical prowess, who has a real vision for where the AI industry is going and who will be able to learn Nvidia's business. I think it's doubtful that person ... Well, actually, I really should have learned from this experience that I shouldn't have hot takes. I was going to say, I don't think the person's going to come from—

Zoë Schiffer: ... Nvidia internally, but what do we know at this point? It could be anything. So yeah, Colette will take the reins for the moment and the company needs to weather this storm and come out the other side.

Brian Barrett: And I think she does, as you might have guessed, she's going to be operating the business the way Jensen had, at least in terms of advocating for closer collaboration with China and the business lines that Nvidia has been going down, leaning more into CPUs recently, open source. I don't think you're going to expect anything other than business as usual. And when you have her in there, as we talked before, China is so important to Nvidia's future. Jensen Huang had lost all credibility on that point. So having someone in there who can make the case that, hey, we should have a better relationship with China, we should be able to sell more to China, who is not compromised in the way that Jensen has been, that's an upside. I mean, it's a lot of downside for Nvidia that Jensen's gone, but at least they can come into these conversations without that baggage. I think that's the main point here.

Leah Feiger: And perhaps even right the ship even slightly. Leah Feiger: It means it's a time for another emergency pod. We're just going to keep this going all day.

Brian Barrett: We're going to have to do another emergency pod. Exactly. All day. Get ready, folks.

Zoë Schiffer: We have been talking a lot though about how interconnected, not just the AI industry is, but the entire US economy, how tied up it is with just a few companies. And Nvidia really is, it's kind of Nvidia and Anthropic that so much of this is riding on. This will be one of the takes, much like Zoë writes her takes, I'm going to regret saying this. I think short term it doesn't do that much. Everyone has their own sort of processor in the smartphone world. It comes at a time when other companies are really trying to find a path to do this without relying so much on Nvidia, and this is only going to accelerate that process.

Zoë Schiffer: Yeah, I mean, but that was true regardless. This isn't a software moment. I think when Elon Musk took over X and suddenly people were fleeing the platform, you saw Meta kind of seize its moment and launch Threads in a few short months. You can't really do that with GPUs. Other companies have been trying to compete with Nvidia for years, and that competition has only heated up as the AI industry has become more and more powerful and there's more and more money to be had. But this is some of the most complex technology on the planet, and so you cannot just see an opportunity and turn it around really, really fast. It takes years of research and manufacturing. And so I think there already was this opportunity for other companies to come in and try and grab some of Nvidia's market share, and I'm sure they will try and capitalize on the moment, but it's not going to be as easy as other examples we've seen in recent history.

Brian Barrett: Oh, for sure. But I think it's more like if you're OpenAI and you're like, "I've been dabbling with making my own chips." Italia, Frattesi: "Obiettivo? Togliermi le etichette. Su Gattuso e Mancini..."

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Davide Frattesi ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni ai microfoni di Rai Sport poco dopo la fine dell'incontro tra Italia e Francia, terminato 3-1 per gli Azzurri

Davide Frattesi ha rilasciato delle dichiarazioni ai microfoni di Rai Sport poco dopo la fine dell'incontro tra Italia e Francia, terminato 3-1 per gli Azzurri.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Una serata emozionante, forse la doppietta con l’Ucraina serviva per un altro scopo però far gol qui è sempre speciale perché in quella porta c’è stato il gol più importante della mia carriera e sono contento di aver segnato questa sera, in questa notte bellissima.

Lazio e Nazionale? Sono due situazioni analoghe, nella Lazio, nonostante la prima sconfitta, siamo riusciti a compattarci e a trovare entusiasmo, credo che quella sia la cosa più importante anche qui grazie al mister e ai ragazzi nuovi che hanno portato una ventata d’aria fresca. Quando c’è un ambiente sano è tutto più facile. Io più che per la vittoria sono contento per il carattere e l’abnegazione che c’è stata.

Con Gattuso abbiamo qualche discussione perché mi chiede di esaltare le caratteristiche che ho, io invece vorrei fare al 100% quello che non so fare. Però quello è sempre stato il mio obiettivo, levarmi le etichette che non rispecchiano quello che sono davvero. Sono contento di aver fiducia da Gattuso e da Mancini.

Quando riesco ad avere quei 10-15 metri e riesco ad accelerare in corsa riesco a coprire una porzione di campo più grande. Come oggi che sono arrivato dietro al difensore, mi sono staccato e ho fatto gol. Per me è importante arrivare da dietro in corsa perché così io posso decidere come attaccare.

Gol contro il Napoli? Quello è merito di Gattuso che ci massacra durante la settimana, la forma fisica c'è.

Mancini? Dopp il gol stavamo parlando della fase difensiva.

What to expect during SC26: Join theCUBE Nov. 17–19

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

High-performance computing and artificial intelligence are converging, driven by advances in exascale computing, accelerated systems, data storage and high-speed networking.

The convergence of these technologies is a prime topic for discussion during SC26 in Chicago this month. Major industry players, such as Dell Technologies Inc., Google LLC and Nvidia Corp., have pursued a set of initiatives in the HPC arena, and SC26 will be an occasion to learn more about how AI and HPC workloads are evolving to write supercomputing’s next chapter.

“SC26 provides an important opportunity to examine how the convergence of AI, high-performance computing and application development are reshaping enterprise infrastructure priorities,” said theCUBE Research’s Paul Nashawaty. “Organizations must connect high-performance infrastructure, accessible data and modern application development to move beyond experimentation and accelerate production-ready AI deployments. For Dell and the broader HPC ecosystem, SC26 offers a platform to demonstrate how integrated compute, storage and AI infrastructure can address these challenges.”

TheCUBE, SiliconANGLE Media’s livestreaming studio, will be covering the latest HPC news and announcements from SC26 in Chicago, Nov. 17–19. Coverage will feature on-site reporting and exclusive interviews with industry leaders and analysts as theCUBE explores how supercomputing’s future is being shaped at the intersection of exascale systems and AI infrastructure. Discussion will focus on ways that accelerated computing is transforming research environments, while quantum-HPC integration and agentic AI push scientific discovery toward greater autonomy. (* Disclosure below.)

Dell’s ongoing collaboration with Nvidia and other organizations has yielded several new developments in the HPC arena over the past year, particularly in the area of quantum computing.

The two companies have been working with the U.S. Department of Energy to build and deliver the Doudna supercomputer at Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory. The system will use Nvidia’s Vera Rubin CPU-GPU platform and Quantum-X800 InfiniBand networking inside Dell’s integrated rack systems and PowerEdge servers to support large-scale scientific computing and data-intensive research.

In September, Dell announced a collaboration with silicon-spin qubit developer Diraq to integrate quantum processing units with HPC and AI server infrastructure. The joint effort is designed to integrate quantum processors with classical computing infrastructure in commercial data centers.

Moves such as these point toward a convergence of AI and quantum technologies, which will likely propel both forward as new platforms are built and deployed.

“The market for high-performance computing is meeting the AI factory at the point of innovation,” said theCUBE Research’s Dave Vellante. “HPC has always led the most challenging data problems, so this trend makes perfect sense. Quantum will be the next big inflection point and we’re seeing that new technologies are often complementary to existing infrastructure. We expect that to be the case between accelerated computing and quantum, particularly in the area of error correction.”

Hyperscaler Google Cloud is also building systems to support HPC demands for industries like manufacturing, health care and life sciences, weather forecasting and electronic design automation.

At the forefront of Google’s HPC initiative is the H4D VM, the company’s high-performance computing virtual machine. Introduced last year, the H4D VM is powered by fifth-generation AMD EPYC processors and offers whole-node VM performance of more than 12,000 GFLOPS and memory bandwidth exceeding 950 GB/s.

An example of how HPC is making inroads in daily life can be seen in the use of H4D VMs by the National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration. In July, Google Cloud announced that it had been selected as the primary provider of high-performance computing infrastructure for the Weather and Climate Operational Supercomputing System.

The adoption of HPC to enable meteorologists and researchers to develop future forecasting models is an example of how systems technology will play a critical role in both supercomputing and AI infrastructure moving forward.

“Systems are the key in supercomputing advances,” said theCUBE Research’s John Furrier. “Different architectures. Different bets. Same underlying problem: How do you keep the multiplication engines doing useful work instead of waiting for data? That’s why I think the next era of AI infrastructure won’t be defined by the ‘best chip.’ It will be defined by the best system architecture.”

While the field of HPC is being defined by major players such as Dell and Google, other firms are making contributions as well in areas such as cloud infrastructure and analytics.

Vultr Inc. is building high-performance cloud computing infrastructure for AI, risk management, compliance and fraud prevention. The company is developing its platform across 33 global regions by leveraging CPUs, GPUs and open, composable architectures to position itself as a cloud infrastructure specialist in the agentic AI era.

High-performance computing is also generating a need for consistent data sharing and access across platforms. Omni Analytics Inc. is building a data collaboration solution that can connect directly to cloud data warehouses without needing to preload or duplicate data.

SC26 will offer an opportunity to not only assess the progress of HPC in the enterprise but also examine how AI is continuing to transform the compute stack to generate meaningful business results.

“As organizations invest in increasingly sophisticated AI environments, the competitive differentiator will be the ability to optimize the entire technology stack from data movement and storage performance to application development and deployment,” Nashawaty said. “The central SC26 narrative is that the future of AI supercomputing will be defined not simply by how much compute organizations deploy, but by how effectively they translate that investment into measurable business and scientific outcomes.”

Don’t miss theCUBE’s coverage of SC26, Nov. 17–19. Plus, you can access theCUBE’s exclusive content on demand after the event.

We offer various ways for you to watch theCUBE’s coverage of SC26, including theCUBE’s dedicated website and YouTube channel. You can also get all the coverage from this year’s events on SiliconANGLE.

SiliconANGLE’s “theCUBE Pod” is available on Apple Podcasts, Spotify and YouTube, which you can enjoy while on the go. During each podcast, SiliconANGLE’s John Furrier and Dave Vellante discuss the biggest trends in enterprise tech, including AI, cloud, cybersecurity and infrastructure, with context and analysis.

SiliconANGLE also produces our weekly “Breaking Analysis” program, where Vellante examines major developments in enterprise tech, combining insights from theCUBE with spending data from Enterprise Technology Research, available on Apple Podcasts, Spotify and YouTube.

At SC26, theCUBE will speak with tech leaders and industry analysts about ways that accelerated computing is transforming research environments, while quantum-HPC integration and agentic AI push scientific discovery toward greater autonomy.

(* Disclosure: TheCUBE is a paid media partner for SC26. Sponsors of theCUBE’s event coverage do not have editorial control over content on theCUBE or SiliconANGLE.)

Support our mission to keep content open and free by engaging with theCUBE community. Join theCUBE’s Alumni Trust Network, where technology leaders connect, share intelligence and create opportunities.

Founded by tech visionaries John Furrier and Dave Vellante, SiliconANGLE Media has built a dynamic ecosystem of industry-leading digital media brands that reach 15+ million elite tech professionals. Our new proprietary theCUBE AI Video Cloud is breaking ground in audience interaction, leveraging theCUBEai.com neural network to help technology companies make data-driven decisions and stay at the forefront of industry conversations.
