9 Developer Tools That Pay for Themselves in a Week

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I tracked what each one saved me. These nine paid back their price in days, not months.

Last Tuesday, at 11:40 at night, Claude Code stopped in the middle of a refactor. Not with an error. With a rate limit. I was three files into a change in Notchy, I had the whole plan in my head, and the tool just said no.

I sat there and did the maths. I'd been paying for that limit with my evenings for months. It never showed up on a bill, only as lost nights.

That night I went through every tool I use to build and ship and asked one question about each: does this pay for itself? Nine of them did, in a week or less. Most are free.

VS Code is the editor and the terminal in one window, and it costs nothing.

I used to keep three apps open to write code: an editor, a terminal, and a browser tab full of docs. Every build meant finding the right window, and half the time I typed the command into the wrong one.

Now the terminal sits underneath the code. I run the build, see the error, fix the line above it. Last month I fixed a crash in Notchy's clipboard code and ran the build forty times in one afternoon. If each run had meant switching apps, I'd still be there.

Checkout here: https://code.visualstudio.com

Privacy: runs on your Mac. It sends some usage telemetry by default, which you can turn off in settings. Your code stays yours.

Raycast is a launcher that does the job of five menu bar apps, and you forget it's there.

Spotlight is fine until you want to do something with the thing you found. I used to open Spotlight, find the app, open it, then go find the file I actually wanted. Three steps for a one-step job. A quick calculation meant opening the Calculator app. For a calculation.

Raycast is one shortcut. Two letters, and there's the app, the file, the command. I launch builds from it, search files, do quick sums. I counted once: about fifty hits a day, ten seconds each. That's eight minutes a day, on a tool I never think about.

Price: free to start. Pro is a subscription, so check the link for the current price. The free version is what I use most days.

Privacy: runs on your Mac, nothing uploaded unless you turn on cloud sync yourself.

GitHub Desktop shows you the diff before you push, so you stop shipping mistakes.

I used to commit from the command line and trust my memory. That worked until the day I pushed a debug print statement and a test file to a release branch, on a Friday. I found out on Monday, from a user.

GitHub Desktop makes the diff the whole point. Before I commit, I see every line that changed. Last month it caught a config change I made while testing and forgot to revert. One line, and it would have broken the build for everyone who pulled it.

Checkout here: https://desktop.github.com

Privacy: runs on your Mac. It talks to GitHub only when you push, pull, or fetch.

DB Browser opens any SQLite database and lets you read it like a spreadsheet.

Guessing what's inside a database is a special kind of miserable. I had a bug once where a count in the app was wrong, and I spent an hour reading code, adding log lines, and rebuilding. The code was fine. The data was wrong. A stale row was sitting in a local database, and I had no way to look at it.

DB Browser is just a window onto the file. Open it, click the table, and there's your data. I found the stale row in about thirty seconds. An hour of guessing, thirty seconds of looking.

Privacy: runs on your Mac, nothing uploaded. Your database never leaves your disk.

Postman lets you poke an API until it confesses, before your code ever touches it.

I used to test APIs by writing the code first and then finding out the endpoint was wrong. That's backwards, and I knew it was backwards, and I did it anyway, because setting up a test felt like work. Then I wired a whole feature to an endpoint that returned a 401 for one kind of request and worked fine for the rest. I found out from a user. Again.

Now I hit the endpoint in Postman first. Last month I was testing a webhook for my referrals site, and Postman showed me the 401 in about ten seconds. Ten seconds, versus a shipped bug and an apology email.

Price: free plan, with paid plans on top. Check the link for the current prices.

Privacy: runs on your Mac. Your requests go only to the API you point it at. Cloud sync is optional, and off until you turn it on.

CleanShot X takes a screenshot, lets you mark it up, and gives you a link. No file to clean up after.

I answer bug reports all day, and one without a picture is a guessing game. Before this, every screenshot meant taking it, opening Preview, cropping it, drawing a circle, saving it, finding it on my Desktop, and attaching it. Six steps, for something I do ten times a day.

Now I take the shot, draw the circle, copy the link, done. Last week a user sent me a screenshot of a crash. I annotated it, sent it back with the fix, and the whole exchange took four minutes. It used to take fifteen.

Price: one-time purchase, no subscription. I'm not quoting the exact number here because it changes with sales, and I'd rather you see the real price on the site than a wrong one from me. Check the link.

Privacy: runs on your Mac. Cloud links are optional, screenshots stay local unless you choose to share one.

Maccy remembers everything you copy, and gives it back with one shortcut.

I copy a crash log, then I copy a link to send someone, and the crash log is gone. That used to happen to me three times a week. I'd go back to the app, find the log again, copy it again, and paste it into a message that was already half written. Sometimes I'd just retype the whole thing from memory, badly.

Maccy keeps a history. Copy three things in a row, hit the shortcut, pull back the first one. That's it. No account, no sync, no fuss. I use it most answering support emails, when I'm copying a log, a link, and a version number in the same minute.

Privacy: runs on your Mac, your clipboard never leaves it. Nothing uploaded.

Rectangle snaps windows into place with one shortcut, and it's free.

I used to drag windows around by hand like it was 2009. Code on the left, docs on the right, and I'd spend a minute every time getting the split right. Then I'd open a third window and ruin it. I did this for years. Dragging windows is not work, it's the opposite of work, and I was doing it ten times a day.

Rectangle is one shortcut. Left half, right half, done. During a refactor I keep code on one side and docs on the other, terminal underneath when I need it. The windows stay put, and I stay in the code.

Privacy: runs on your Mac, nothing uploaded.

Notchy puts your AI usage in the notch, so you see the limit coming before it hits.

This is the one from Tuesday night. Claude Code stopped mid-refactor, and I had no warning at all. I didn't know how much of my session was left, or when it reset, or what it had cost me. I was flying blind on a tool I use for hours every day.

Notchy reads the usage window from Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, and Copilot and shows it right in the notch: tokens, cost, and how much of the window is left, with an alert before you hit the limit. Now I glance up and know if I have twenty minutes left or two hours. If the window is nearly gone, I save the big refactor for after the reset.

That one change gave me back a dead evening a week, from one glance at the notch.

Price: €14.99 one-time until Nov 30, then €24.99. 7-day free trial. No subscription. Version 2.0.8.

Privacy: runs on your Mac, nothing uploaded. No account needed for the trial. It reads your local CLI logs, or a provider API key you store in the Keychain.

Add it all up. Seven of these nine are free. One is a one-time purchase, one has a free plan with paid tiers. The only price I can quote you exactly is mine: €14.99 until the end of November. The whole stack costs less than one dinner out, and it pays for itself in a week.

The expensive tools aren't the ones you buy. They're the ones you don't buy, and then you pay for them with your evenings.

Which one are you installing first? Tell me in the comments. And if a tool pays for itself in your week, I want to hear about it. I read every reply.

I'm Vishva. I make Notchy, a Dynamic Island for your Mac, and Peekaboo, a Mac app for finding files in plain English and freeing space with undo. I write about Mac apps and AI tools that actually save time. Follow if you want the next list.

POS Inventory Management Software: How It Works & Benefits

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Retail businesses handle hundreds or even thousands of product transactions every day. Managing sales, inventory, billing, and stock updates separately can lead to errors, delays, and inefficient operations.

This is where POS inventory management software becomes valuable.

By connecting point-of-sale transactions with inventory tracking, retailers can maintain more accurate stock records, simplify daily operations, and make better purchasing decisions.

In this article, we'll explore how POS inventory management software works, its essential features, and what retailers should consider before choosing a solution.

POS inventory management software combines a point-of-sale system with inventory tracking capabilities.

A POS system records customer purchases, processes supported payments, and generates receipts. Inventory management software monitors product quantities, stock movements, and replenishment requirements.

When these systems are integrated, sales transactions can automatically update inventory records.

For example, if a retail store has 100 units of a product and sells 8 units, an integrated POS system can update the available stock to 92 units.

This reduces duplicate data entry and improves inventory visibility.

A typical POS inventory workflow involves several steps.

Retailers add products to the system with details such as:

When a customer purchases a product, the POS system records the transaction and calculates the total amount.

After a completed sale, the inventory system reduces the available stock quantity.

Initial stock: 50 units Quantity sold: 5 units Remaining stock: 45 units

Retailers can review inventory reports to identify products with low stock levels.

Depending on the software, low-stock alerts or reorder recommendations can help retailers plan future purchases.

Real-time inventory tracking helps businesses monitor product quantities as sales and stock movements occur.

This improves stock visibility and reduces manual tracking requirements.

Barcode scanning can simplify product identification and speed up checkout operations.

It also helps reduce mistakes caused by manually entering product information.

An integrated POS inventory system can automatically adjust stock quantities after completed sales, returns, and other supported inventory transactions.

Low-stock notifications help retailers identify products that may need replenishment.

Reports help store owners understand product performance, inventory turnover, and purchasing patterns.

Some POS platforms support inventory tracking across multiple store locations.

This can help growing retail businesses manage stock availability across branches.

Role-based permissions can help businesses control who can view, modify, or manage inventory records.

From a software development perspective, a POS inventory system typically includes several components.

Frontend: Provides interfaces for billing, product management, and inventory dashboards.

Backend: Handles business logic, transaction processing, inventory updates, and reporting.

Database: Stores products, sales records, stock quantities, and transaction history.

API layer: Connects frontend applications, backend services, and external integrations.

Customer Purchase → POS Application → Backend API → Sales Database → Inventory Update → Stock Report

For reliable inventory updates, systems should use appropriate transaction handling to avoid inconsistent records.

This basic example illustrates inventory deduction. A production POS application would also need database transactions, concurrency controls, error handling, and persistent storage.

These benefits depend on the software's capabilities and how effectively it is implemented.

Before choosing a solution, retailers should evaluate their operational needs.

Comparing these factors helps businesses identify software that matches their requirements.

Businesses researching retail POS technology can explore AvenuePOS.

Visit the official website to review its current software offerings and verify whether its inventory management, billing, and reporting capabilities meet your requirements.

Choosing a suitable POS solution can help retailers connect important business operations and improve daily efficiency.

POS inventory management software helps connect retail sales with stock tracking, reducing repetitive manual work and improving inventory visibility.

From automated stock updates to sales reporting, an integrated system can help businesses manage products more efficiently.

For retailers and developers alike, understanding how POS inventory systems work is an important step toward building smarter and more reliable retail operations.

Integrating EURC: Euro Stablecoin Traps for Developers (Plus an On-Chain Check in Python)

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

If you have only ever integrated dollar stablecoins, EURC looks familiar: same issuer as USDC, a USDC-style smart contract, six decimals. But a euro stablecoin brings a few traps that dollar-coin code never had to handle. This post walks through them from a developer's point of view, with a small Python script that reads the token straight from Ethereum.

For the non-technical background (who issues it, what backs it, fees, where to buy it and how it's taxed), see this plain-English explainer on EURC, Circle's euro stablecoin.

EURC is a euro-pegged token issued by Circle's French subsidiary as an e-money token under the EU's MiCA rules. Each token is meant to be worth €1 and redeemable 1:1, with reserves held as euro bank deposits. It pays holders no interest and lives on several chains, including Ethereum, Base, Solana, Avalanche and Stellar. If stablecoins are new to you, start with how stablecoins work.

The most common bug is a units bug. Plenty of dashboards and wallets quietly assume every stablecoin equals one US dollar. EURC tracks the euro, so its USD value moves with EUR/USD every day.

The same applies to accounting: for users outside the eurozone, swapping EURC back to their home currency can create a gain or loss purely from FX movement. Our guide to how stablecoins are taxed covers why that matters.

The token used to be called "Euro Coin" with the ticker EUROC. Circle renamed it but kept the same contract addresses, so some explorers and apps still show the old name. That is exactly why you should never identify a token by its name or symbol: anyone can deploy a contract called "EURC". Identify it by contract address, taken from Circle's developer docs, and pin it in config per chain.

Like USDC, EURC on Ethereum uses 6 decimals, not 18. Hard-coding 18 is a classic bug that turns €5 into a rounding error. Read decimals() once, store it next to the address, and do arithmetic with integers or Decimal, never floats.

Also remember that supply is split across chains. The Ethereum contract only knows about Ethereum.

This script calls a public Ethereum RPC and reads the four standard ERC-20 views. It uses only the standard library. Double-check the address against Circle's docs before relying on it.

When I ran it on November 12, 2026, it printed:

Two things to notice. The on-chain name() still returns "Euro Coin", which is Trap 2 in action. And the Ethereum supply is well below the roughly 471 million EURC that CoinGecko showed across all chains that day, because the rest lives on other networks. If you report "total supply", you need to sum each chain you support, or use an aggregator and say so.

Under MiCA, holders have a right to redeem at par, and Circle offers a direct redemption path for retail holders. In practice it requires identity checks and an EEA bank account that receives SEPA payments. If your users are in the US, Canada or Australia, their realistic exit is selling on an exchange or swapping on a DEX, so liquidity and spreads matter more than the redemption promise. For a broader view of what can go wrong, see whether stablecoins are actually safe.

This post is for education only and is not financial advice.

This post was written with AI assistance.

Axis Robotics' ODE Policy Threads a Nut Onto a Bolt, With Three More Assembly Skills Queued

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 12 • Originally published at x.com

Axis Robotics has shipped ODE Policy, a visual policy distillation method that teaches a Franka robot to thread a nut onto a bolt in simulation — the first of four assembly skills the company says it intends to convert into policies that act from camera images. The method is built around learning how actions evolve over time, an approach the company argues gives a distilled policy a more natural way to capture the continuity of robot motion. Axis Robotics describes the early results as strong.

https://x.com/i/status/2120078708879102220

Visual policy distillation is the practice of taking a policy that already solves a task — typically one with access to privileged information such as full simulation state — and compressing it into a student policy that must act from camera images alone. The student is what ships, because cameras are cheap, non-invasive and already present on most industrial cells. The teacher is what makes training tractable, because simulation can supply ground truth that a real cell cannot.

ODE Policy's contribution, as described in the company's own account, is temporal. Rather than supervising the student on isolated action labels, it learns how actions evolve over time, so the policy models the trajectory of motion rather than a sequence of independent decisions. That distinction matters most in contact-rich tasks, where the correct action at any instant depends heavily on the momentum and contact state established a moment earlier. Threading a nut onto a bolt is a compact example: the tolerance is tight, the failure modes are subtle, and a policy that reacts only to the current frame tends to oscillate or stall.

The demonstration reproduces a task the company attributes to SGS, run in simulation on a Franka arm. Axis Robotics says the next step is to widen the set of skills distilled this way, naming gear fitting, peg insertion and connector joining alongside the nut-threading result.

The company's own figures describe a narrow proof of concept and the broader plan behind it.

The ratio is the interesting part. One skill is shown; three more are named in the same breath. Those three are not variations on a theme — gear fitting, peg insertion and connector joining are distinct contact regimes with different failure modes, different tooling and different tolerance budgets. A distillation method that generalises across all four would be doing something meaningfully different from a method tuned to one task.

Contact-rich assembly is the part of factory automation that has resisted the economics of scale. Welding, painting and palletising became commodity robot applications because the motion is repeatable and the sensing requirements are modest. Inserting a peg or seating a connector is not: it demands force awareness, compliance and error recovery, and the traditional answer has been bespoke engineering per part, per fixture, per tolerance. That is why assembly cells remain expensive to commission and slow to change over.

If a policy can be distilled from simulation into a camera-only controller, the marginal cost of adding a new skill shifts from mechanical engineering to data generation and training. Cameras replace instrumented fixtures. Simulation replaces physical trial and error. The cost curve bends in the direction that has made every other branch of machine learning cheaper: more skills per unit of engineering, not more engineering per skill.

The competitive implication is a squeeze on two fronts. Generalist robot foundation model efforts argue that one large policy should absorb many tasks; task-specific distillation argues that narrow, well-supervised policies will reach production reliability first. Axis Robotics is placing a bet on the second, with a method that tries to import the temporal structure of motion rather than relearning it from scratch.

The open question is the one the demo does not answer. Everything shown runs in simulation, and the sim-to-real gap is where contact-rich policies have historically failed — friction models, sensor noise, and the small deformations that simulation approximates poorly. Distilling to camera images is the right target, but evidence that it survives contact with hardware will come from the next set of skills, not this one.

Neverness to Everness, Wuthering Waves, Duet Night Abyss - We've Gacha Covered

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Love 'em or hate 'em, gacha RPGs are everywhere - so much so that they dominate our mailboxes on most days since they're just filled to the brim with all sorts of shiny new things (and I do love shiny new things). Whether it's a fancy SSR character joining the lineup or a cool new region to explore, we've got a nifty list of updates here where we go through what's caught our eye so far!  Neverness to Everness I feel like Neverness to Everness's latest update is pretty timely, as Version 1.5 is aptly dubbed "Once Upon a Glitched Call". From AI Native Advertising to Marketing System Delivery: BlueMedia launches partnership with ChatGPT Ads

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SHANGHAI, Nov. 12, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Recently, BlueMedia officially launched its partnership with ChatGPT Ads. The partnership will focus on helping Chinese brands enter AI-powered conversational and decision-making scenarios while exploring innovative applications of AI Native advertising in global marketing.

As ChatGPT becomes increasingly embedded in how people search for information, compare products, make purchasing decisions, and choose services, generative AI is emerging as a new gateway connecting user needs with commercial information. ChatGPT now has more than 1.2 billion weekly active users globally. ChatGPT Ads first launched in the U.S. market in February this year and has since gradually expanded to more than 89 markets across North America, Europe, Asia, Oceania, the Middle East, and North Africa.

As a leading China-based provider of international marketing services, BlueMedia brings extensive experience in global marketing, having served more than 100,000 clients across key industries, including consumer electronics, gaming, e-commerce, internet services, and automotive. With local service networks established in markets including the United States, Singapore, Vietnam, Thailand, the Netherlands, Brazil, Indonesia, the United Kingdom, and Malaysia, BlueMedia has built an integrated service model that combines global media resources, industry expertise, and local market operations. Guided by its "AI + Globalization 2.0" strategy, BlueMedia continues to advance its AI Native transformation, integrating AI technologies into market intelligence, creative production, media buying, and performance optimization, while expanding the application of AI across real-world marketing scenarios.

The adoption of AI capabilities at scale is also becoming a critical foundation for BlueMedia's global marketing services. Powered by the Blue AI technology platform and its suite of products, BlueMedia continues to extend AI beyond individual tools and into end-to-end marketing workflows, progressively advancing toward higher levels of intelligence characterized by autonomous decision-making and execution. BlueFocus currently has more than 1,000 AI agents deployed in live business operations. Within its international marketing business, revenue from AI-driven operations involving long-running tasks completed through A2A (Agent-to-Agent) interactions and autonomous AI decision-making has exceeded RMB 300 million. By advancing from AI capability development to deployment at scale in real business environments, BlueMedia is building an AI-driven marketing services ecosystem that also provides a stronger foundation for the commercial deployment of emerging advertising formats such as ChatGPT Ads.

To address user needs and commercial conversion pathways within AI-powered conversational experiences, BlueMedia has combined its Blue AI technology capabilities with its global marketing expertise to build a full-stack AI marketing service framework covering user intent insights, product analysis, advertising strategy, context hints optimization, landing page review and development, data monitoring, ad campaign execution, and performance analysis. This framework enables BlueMedia to deliver end-to-end advertising solutions tailored to ChatGPT Ads. BlueMedia has already launched ChatGPT Ads campaigns for its first group of brand clients, with preliminary data indicating that campaign performance is exceeding industry averages. In parallel, BlueMedia has introduced tailored solutions for industries including e-commerce, travel, and AI applications, addressing the distinct business needs of different markets and brands. As these initiatives progress, BlueMedia will continue to refine its standardized advertising service framework powered by Blue AI, build on campaign experience across markets and industries, and advance AI native advertising from early adoption toward deployment at scale.

BlueMedia aims to work with ChatGPT Ads to connect AI Native advertising, model capabilities, and marketing systems, using long-term data feedback and real-world business practice to help AI continuously learn and improve in actual marketing scenarios, while enabling brands to build sustainable, long-term AI marketing capabilities across global markets.

The value of AI goes beyond improving efficiency. More fundamentally, it is reshaping how marketing is produced, delivered, and monetized. Globalization, in turn, provides a much broader range of real-world scenarios for AI Native capabilities. Looking ahead, BlueMedia will continue to deepen its "AI + Globalization" capabilities, embed AI more deeply into brands' global marketing systems, and explore more frontier AI Native marketing scenarios around the real needs of Chinese companies going global, accelerating its journey toward becoming a truly global AI marketing technology company.

Michael Carrick’s future as Manchester United manager is in severe doubt after overseeing the club’s worst start to the Premier League season in 40 years that has left them in the relegation zone, with Mauricio Pochettino looking set to replace him.

A second Old Trafford comeback of the week, this time battling from two goals down twice to rescue a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa on Sunday, might not be enough to save Carrick’s job and he is at risk of being sacked after just 10 games of the season and with his side 18th during the international break.

United ended a nine-game winless run when they came from two goals down to beat Roma in the Champions League last week but they have not won in eight attempts in the Premier League and have just one top-flight win this campaign.

Carrick survived crisis talks with the United hierarchy after defeats to Como and Bournemouth last month and was given time to turn results around, but speculation over a change begun the day after Sunday’s chaotic draw and The Independent understands contact was initially made with United States boss Pochettino over succeeding Carrick on Monday.

The Old Trafford hierarchy, including Jason Wilcox, Omar Berrarda and Sir Jim Ratcliffe, have met in Monaco to discuss Carrick’s future and the club are on the verge of hashing out a deal with Pochettino, who is reportedly ready to accept the position if United ditch their current boss.

Follow latest updates from Old Trafford here:

Mauricio Pochettino began his managerial career at Espanyol before moving to Southampton for a 16-month spell in charge.

The Argentine was then appointed as manager of Tottenham in the summer of 2014 and established them as a top four club, taking them to the Champions League final in 2019 before being dismissed less than six months afterwards.

He won three trophies as manager of Paris Saint-Germain including the 2021/22 Ligue 1 title.

In 2023/24 he spent a season as Chelsea boss before being fired in the summer.

Pochettino then replaced Gregg Berhalter as United States Men’s National Team head coach in 2024, following the side’s group-stage exit from the Copa America. He has won 21 of his 35 matches in charge and took the nation to the last-16 of the World Cup this summer.

Peter Schmeichel has accused Manchester United of "disrespectful" treatment towards Michael Carrick as rumours linking Mauricio Pochettino to the club persist.

Former United keeper Schmeichel believes the root problems lie above Carrick, arguing the head coach should be allowed to sign players in the January window.

"I absolutely do feel sympathy for him. I think the way this is playing out is very disrespectful to him," Schmeichel told The Overlap Daily.

"There are so many things that have been going on at Manchester United literally since Sir Alex (Ferguson) and David Gill left the club. And it’s never been corrected. It’s not been the correct people, not the right people in charge, and slowly it’s just gone worse.”

Manchester United have reportedly struck a verbal agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to take over as head coach in the event of Michael Carrick’s sacking.

Carrick took training on Wednesday but it expected to be relieved of his duties before the end of the week, with Pochettino ready to accept United’s proposal.

The Argentinian, who has previously managed in the Premier League for Chelsea, Tottenham and Southampton, will likely remain in charge of the USA for their two games against Haiti during the current international break.

On Sunday evening, Mauricio Pochettino got the call he had been waiting for.

He and long-time assistant Jesus Perez had frequently asked about Manchester United over the last decade. They would often contact friends in the game who were connected to Old Trafford, looking to find out what was happening, whether there could be an opening.

Pochettino came very close to taking one of those openings on at least three occasions: 2018, 2021 and 2024. Now, elements might finally have aligned.

US Soccer was prepared for the prospect of Mauricio Pochettino leaving the United States before the next World Cup, The Independent understands.

A succession plan has been drawn up in the event that Pochettino’s release clause is activated, with Man United thought to be ready to pay approximately £7m for the Argentine coach and his support staff.

The USA face a double header against Haiti during this international break, though it remains unclear if an interim boss will lead the stars and stripes before a long-term successor to Pochettino is confirmed.

USA defender Antonee Robinson says head coach Mauricio Pochettino is focused on their upcoming matches amid links with Manchester United.

Robinson said: "That news kind of got sprung on all of us, like the rest of the world, I'd say. And similar things happened in the past, happened at the World Cup, where huge teams lose their manager and they start looking at possible candidates.

"And for us to have a manager who's linked with those sorts of jobs means we're very lucky, and we know that we're working with a top-class manager.

"But that never seems to leak into the work we do, whether it was the World Cup or whether it's going to be this camp. All that sort of outside noise is nothing to do with him.

"He's very much focused on getting the best out of the team and getting these two wins in the Nations League, and we'll be on the same page as that."

Should Carrick remain in charge or should he be replaced whoever is in the dugout following the international break will have a tricky start.

United’s next fixture is against Liverpool in the Premier League before a run of games against clubs that could firmly place them in a relegation battle should they fail to win them.

Liverpool (A) - Premier League - Sunday November 22

Sporting (A) - Champions League - Wednesday November 25

Brentford (H) - Premier League - Saturday November 28

Newcastle (A) - Premier League - Wednesday December 2

Coventry (H) - Premier League - Saturday December 5

RB Leipzig (H) - Champions League - Tuesday December 8

Following the end of last season and Man Utd’s third place finish in the Premier League hope was high for a positive season in 2026/27.

The club were back in the Champions League and Michael Carrick had the players firing on the pitch but things didn’t turn out as planned.

A 2-0 defeat to newly promoted Hull City on the opening day of the season kicked off the campaign with the worst possible start and things declined from there.

United’s 5-2 victory over Ipswich was peppered with mistakes while a draw against Everton and then Sabah in the Champions League offered some hope.

That Sabah win came on September 10th and United did not win another game until November 3rd which translated to a nine-match winless run.

In that time they lost to rivals Man City - who played the majority of the game with 10-men - and were knocked out of the Carabao Cup by Brighton despite being 2-0 up at Old Trafford.

A 4-1 defeat to Como in Europe then kick started a run of games which saw United conced 15 goals in five games. Three of those ended in defeats with one win and one draw.

The latter are United’s two most recent matches which were both fraught affairs.

United came from 2-0 down against Roma to triumph 3-2 in the Champions League while the Red Devils twice overcame a two goal deficit against Aston Villa to secure a 4-4 draw in the league last Sunday.

Manchester United have made their worst start to a season in 40 years under Michael Carrick this year.

They’ve won just once in the Premier League, against Ipswich Town, and have gained only eight points from the first 10 matches.

Pressure has mounted increasingly on Carrick over recent weeks though United are unbeaten in their last two games with a 3-2 win over Roma in the Champions League followed by a 4-4 draw against Aston Villa on Sunday.

That win over Roma is United’s only victory, across all competitions, in their last 11 games.

Good morning and welcome to live updates on the turmoil around Manchester United.

The Independent’s Chief Football Writer Miguel Delaney reports that the club are close to an agreement with Mauricio Pochettino to become their next manager.

It is not yet rubber-stamped that Michael Carrick will be sacked, but that is the expectation following a meeting of the hierarchy in Monaco yesterday, and Pochettino is now lined up as a replacement.

We've Witnessed the Stock Market Do This 19 Times in 76 Years, and Equities Have Been Higher One Year Later 100% of the Time

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Past events have a knack for forecasting the future on Wall Street.

This has shaped up as another stellar year for the iconic Dow Jones Industrial Average (^DJI +0.49%), benchmark S&P 500 (^GSPC +1.31%), and growth-focused Nasdaq Composite (^IXIC +1.60%). The artificial intelligence (AI) infrastructure build-out, better-than-expected corporate earnings, and record S&P 500 share buybacks have collectively lifted all three stock indexes to several new highs.

But recent volatility also suggests that equities may be on shaky ground. Just two days after the midterm elections, on Nov. 5, the Nasdaq Composite logged its largest-ever single-day point decline (1,422 points).

While headwinds are clearly mounting on Wall Street, historical precedent offers a different take.

Election Day went, more or less, as the prediction markets had forecast. Democrats swept both houses of Congress, setting up a scenario in which few major spending bills will be signed into law from January 2027 through January 2029.

But there was one surprise on Election Night that clearly unnerved professional and everyday investors. Democrats flipped five governorships previously held by Republicans (with two races still uncalled by the Associated Press, as of Nov. 9), including in Texas, Ohio, and Georgia. These three states are critical to the AI data center build-out. Whereas President Donald Trump favors as little regulation as possible for AI, most Democratic lawmakers want to limit data center expansion and ramp up the vetting of large language models.

Gina Hinojosa may not be as bad for Texas data centers as it looks. Her plan asks for: no subsidies, union labor, no NDAs, and proof household power and water bills won't rise. Much of that is already underway: Amazon, Google, Meta, Microsoft, OpenAI, Oracle and xAI pledged in March to bring their own power and pay for transmission Microsoft pledged to pay full property taxes without seeking tax breaks Nvidia is working with Vistra, NextEra and Constellation on data centers that cut power use when the grid is strained

The stock market entered 2026 at its second-highest valuation in history. A market that's priced for perfection can ill afford a slowdown in the AI infrastructure build-out.

The ideological differences between President Trump and the Democrat-led Congress will likely also make debt-ceiling negotiations more challenging.

If the Dow, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite are to reach new highs, they'll have to climb the proverbial wall of worry.

Although no predictive tool can ever guarantee what the Dow, S&P 500, and Nasdaq Composite will do over the short term, some past events have a knack for foreshadowing the future. One, in particular, has a flawless track record, dating back to 1950.

According to data from Morgan Stanley (MS +1.63%), the S&P 500 has been higher at the six- and 12-month marks following each of the previous 19 midterm elections since 1950.

Carson Group's Chief Market Strategist Ryan Detrick also notes that November has been green for the S&P 500 100% of the time following midterm elections over the last 76 years.

S&P 500 is higher 6 and 12 months after every midterm election over the last 75 years 📈🤑🥳 https://t.co/N1Eu0fZDie

Despite all the pre- and post-election noise that investors have been attempting to digest, history shows that stocks consistently head higher after midterm elections, regardless of how the political puzzle pieces are arranged. This data serves as a good reminder that factors such as corporate operating results, capital-return programs, management decisions, and innovation matter far more in the grand scheme of things on Wall Street than the makeup of Congress over the next two years.

Will there be stock market hiccups in the second half of Trump's term? More than likely, yes.

But have the midterm elections materially changed the long-term outlook for equities? A resounding no.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 835%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 208% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Why Is Tim Cook Being Mentioned as Nvidia’s Next CEO?

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Crypto Market News » Why Is Tim Cook Being Mentioned as Nvidia’s Next CEO?

As prediction markets price in a Jensen Huang exit, Yale’s Jeffrey Sonnenfeld names an unlikely outside successor.

Former Apple CEO Tim Cook has entered the conversation as a potential successor to Jensen Huang after Yale professor Jeffrey Sonnenfeld floated his name in Time magazine, citing Cook’s unique track record following a major tech founder.

Now serving as Apple’s executive chairman after stepping down in September, Cook is drawing speculative interest on prediction markets as reports surrounding Huang’s personal life and an alleged Chinese intelligence link spark boardroom scrutiny at the world’s largest chipmaker.

When Sonnenfeld weighed potential outside replacements alongside former Google CEO Eric Schmidt, Cook stood out for several practical reasons that mirror Nvidia’s current position:

Still, there is no indication that Nvidia has approached Cook or that he is actively seeking the job. And to understand why succession is even being discussed, it helps to look at the controversy unfolding inside Nvidia’s boardroom.

Since November 8, media reports have alleged that Huang had an affair with a woman suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence—a relationship he reportedly acknowledged in a letter to staff.

The revelations have raised immediate questions around national security risks and executive succession. In response, Nvidia’s board launched an independent investigation, sending Nvidia shares down more than 8% in after-hours trading.

While the board investigates, prediction market traders are already betting aggressively on how soon Huang could step down—and who might replace him.

On Polymarket, traders are giving an 88% chance that Huang leaves his position by December 31, with the year-end contract drawing more than $4.7 million in volume.

Bettors also place 59% odds (across $2.43 million in volume) on an announcement happening as soon as today, November 12. On Kalshi, 63% of bettors expect Huang to depart by November 15.

Trading is equally active in the successor markets, though insiders currently lead the pack.

On Kalshi current Nvidia CFO Colette Kress leads at 30%, closely followed by Ian Buck, who runs Nvidia’s hyperscale and high-performance computing business, at 29%. Tim Cook sits at 5%.

On Polymarket, Buck holds the top spot at 25%, just ahead of Kress at 22%.

Because Nvidia sits at the center of the global AI trade, sudden leadership uncertainty ripples far beyond a single stock. Alongside Nvidia’s after-hours drop, broader tech equities and AI-linked crypto assets, including Nvidia-linked crypto derivatives and tokenized stocks, have already posted losses as markets brace for what comes next.

LinkedIn brings its B2B ad targeting to U.K. connected TVs

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

In its latest effort to capitalize on growing demand for video, LinkedIn is expanding its CTV ads to the U.K.

The launch follows its staged U.S. rollout, which began in April 2024, before becoming widely available across the U.S. and Canada in 2025. It brings LinkedIn’s professional audience targeting to CTV, allowing advertisers to reach U.K. decision-makers by job role, industry and seniority while they watch ad-supported streaming content. For example, a software company looking to reach senior IT decision-makers could use LinkedIn’s targeting to serve CTV ads to viewers in relevant job roles and industries, rather than relying solely on broader demographic or household-level targeting.

So far, the platform has partnered with LG and Titan OS for its U.K. CTV ads offering, and it’s available to all advertisers in the market.

“When you think about professional identities and how you want to reach and engage with them, we’re a platform where people tell us who they are,” said Jae Oh, senior director of product management at LinkedIn. “They get verified for the jobs that they have, and we’re able to support a higher fidelity of reach when it comes to how you reach those audiences at scale.”

LinkedIn is positioning CTV as more than just an awareness play, arguing that its professional audience targeting can help advertisers reach decision-makers earlier in the buying process and influence them further down the funnel.

“For so long CTV has been seen as like you naturally think it’s an awareness play, but actually where we’ve seen a real success is when clients use it as part of a full-funnel strategy,” said Milka Privodanova, vice president of LinkedIn Marketing Solutions, large customers — international at LinkedIn.

The company claims that people exposed to a brand’s CTV ad cost 30% less to convert into sales leads. Cisco, which has already tested the targeting update, saw 52% incremental reach beyond its LinkedIn in-feed activity, 141% higher lead form completion rates and 35% higher click-through rates among audiences exposed to its CTV ads, according to its LinkedIn case study.

But the proposition comes with questions over cost and targeting accuracy. Accuracast, for example, evaluated LinkedIn’s CTV offering when it was only available in the U.S. and found its pricing significantly higher than other direct CTV providers, alongside high minimum spend requirements.

“There’s definitely a business case for it — our B2B clients would love to get more reach and eyeballs on connected TVs — but if we’re going to pay LinkedIn’s usual premium pricing, we’d want more clarity on how LinkedIn plans to accurately target their users on these devices,” said the agency’s group CEO Farhad Divecha.

The idea is that advertisers can use CTV to introduce their brand to decision-makers, retarget and nurture those audiences through video and other LinkedIn ad formats to encourage them to take action. The platform is also looking to improve how advertisers measure CTV’s contribution to business outcomes beyond clicks and lead form completions. Oh said LinkedIn is also investing in revenue and pipeline attribution, alongside incrementality testing, to help advertisers understand CTV’s contribution to business outcomes.

“You will see deeper investment when it comes to incrementality testing or experimentation to prove that these different tactics you’re employing work for you,” Oh said.

The U.K. CTV expansion gives LinkedIn another way to tap into the demand for video, by  extending its video offering beyond the platform and onto the TV screen.

Video advertising on LinkedIn is growing 25% year-over-year, according to Privodanova, though she declined to share how much CTV contributes to that growth.

LinkedIn has been stitching together the pieces of its CTV business since 2024 when it lined up Roku and Samsung Ads as supply partners and launch LinkedIn Premier, a managed product built on NBCUniversal’s streaming inventory, A deal for premium Paramount inventory followed in mid-2025, along with integrations with Innovid and Sprinkly meant to make CTV campaigns easier to run.

“As a premium advertising platform, LinkedIn will need to demonstrate that its audience data can translate into meaningful reach and measurable impact,” said Andy Hunt, head of paid media at agency Brave Bison. “Targeting professionals through household devices presents its own challenges around signal quality, accuracy and attribution. Advertisers will want to understand how effectively CTV exposure can be connected to wider campaign performance, rather than simply adding another awareness metric.”

TikTok Shop is offering the shopping service to more than 1 million people it has identified as its most loyal U.S. customers.

Humble Bundle Undergoes A Vibecoded-Looking Redesign, Forgets To Implement Full Account Security

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Humble Bundle, the PC storefront behind various charity game bundles, has been updated with what appears to be an all-new vibe-coded redesign, and it seems to be extremely flawed. As a few people have noted, the update appears to have stripped the site of various security features that could put user accounts and saved credit card information at risk.

Initially discovered by users on Reddit, the update has entirely removed the option for two-factor authenticationâwhich appears to be true for all users: when logging into my own account, the choice to add or remove the feature is entirely missing under security settings. People are reporting myriad other issues, including errors preventing their purchasing new bundles. Various standard security features are missing following the new update: there are no authentication checks when changing passwords or email addresses, potentially making it easier for anyone to gain control of an account without a user’s permission. If logged in you can simply enter a new password without having to know the current one. Additionally, when attempting to change your email addresses, no verification is sent to the old email address, with information only being directed towards the newly-inputted email account. We’ve tested this too, and incredibly it’s the case.

Beyond security issues, many have complained about a host of features previously on the site being removed or obfuscated in the shift to “vibe-coded brainrot.” Some have noticed that there’s no longer differentiation between redeemed and unredeemed games and codes, making it harder for users with large libraries to sort through their purchase history and find unclaimed games. Thereâs also no way to remain logged-in, forcing users to log in every time they access their library. Others simply dislike the new design as appearing reminiscent of other Claude interfaces.

Though some are hoping for a return to the old design, many are more notably concerned by the security issues introduced with this update. For now, itâs recommended to remove any payment information saved to your Humble account in case of unauthorized access, at least until these issues are resolved. Megabonk's Unhinged New Replacement Is Officially Free On Steam Now

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Megabonk was one of the most surprising and unexpected hits of 2025, though despite its meteoric popularity, the game's developer has more or less abandoned it after the last Halloween update that introduced a new map, character, and boss. That said, Megabonk's simple premise, bizarre yet addictive gameplay, and infinite replayability inspired an avalanche of similarly styled indie games.

While Megabonk-likes have taken the bullet-heaven premise to the extremes and experimented with all sorts of different genres, mechanics, etc., there's one game in particular that fans of vedinad's hit will absolutely not want to skip. Better yet, the game is entirely free to try on Steam starting today, November 12, 2026, thanks to a newly launched demo.

If Megabonk wasn't already wild enough for you, there's a new Steam game that takes the same basic premise and cranks things up several notches. Developed by Aquila Games, Together, Against Mork combines the ridiculous combat of a bullet-heaven roguelike with tower-defense mechanics, giving players even more ways to deal with overwhelming hordes of enemies. Best of all, a free demo for Together, Against Mork is officially available on Steam starting November 12, letting players experience the action without spending a dime.

At its core, Together, Against Mork shares plenty of DNA with Megabonk, with players fighting off enormous waves of enemies, stacking upgrades, and experimenting with increasingly powerful builds. However, its biggest selling point is the addition of an entirely separate tower-defense system that introduces a twist to the mix and an entirely new strategic element to the carnage.

According to the game's official Steam page, there are three distinct modes to choose from:

Beyond these modes, Aquila Games is promising an impressive amount of content for the full release, including seven unique maps, more than 30 enemy types, and over 20 playable characters. Challenges, rewards, and permanent upgrades should also give players plenty of reasons to return for another run. The developers even describe the experience as "insanely chaotic," promising game-breaking synergies and last-second Hail Mary victories.

While the full version of Together, Against Mork has yet to receive a confirmed release date, its free Steam demo gives Megabonk fans an opportunity to try a promising new alternative, particularly for anyone who enjoys a little strategy mixed in with their roguelike mayhem.

“I had to learn to live with that pain, because the dressing room needed the best version of me” Mauricio Pochettino on the toughest period of his football career

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The death of a player is an awful time for all concerned, of course, and Mauricio Pochettino had to try and handle that just a few months into his managerial career

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Mauricio Pochettino is hopefully unlikely to ever again to face anything in his managerial career that is as horrible as the death of one of his players.

The Argentinian gaffer was less than a year into his first head coach role at Espanyol in August 2009 when 26-year-old defender Dani Jarque suffered a fatal heart attack during a pre-season trip to Florence.

Espanyol captain Jarque was on the phone to his pregnant girlfriend in his hotel room at the time.

Pochettino confirmed to FourFourTwo that period was the toughest experience of his life, saying: "Without doubt. It affected all of us deeply.

"I was one of the last people to see him alive, at the team hotel in Coverciano.

"We were having lunch – it was a Saturday and I’d given the players the afternoon off to go and explore such a beautiful city as Florence.

"Jarque told us he had a slight headache and that he wasn’t going out. The doctor gave him a paracetamol and left.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

"It was something that changed our perspective on priorities, although time does become an ally.

"It helps you heal, even if never completely. I had to learn to live with that pain, because the dressing room needed the best version of me."

Much as it obviously paled into insignificance, Pochettino went on to guide the grieving club to a mid-table finish that season, having led them away from the drop zone following his arrival midway through the previous campaign.

Espanyol paid tribute to Jarque as they won their first game at their new stadium just a few weeks after his death, and their training ground is now named after him.

His former Spain youth team teammates Andres Iniesta and Cesc Fabregas went on to wear shirts dedicated to Jarques memory during their victory in the 2010 World Cup final and following the national side's Euro 2012 triumph.

I’ve tested TextNow, Google Fi, and Visible — here’s the unlimited cell phone plan I recommend

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Here at Tom’s Guide our expert editors are committed to bringing you the best news, reviews and guides to help you stay informed and ahead of the curve!

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Sign up to get the latest updates on all of your favorite content! From cutting-edge tech news and the hottest streaming buzz to unbeatable deals on the best products and in-depth reviews, we’ve got you covered.

Be AI savvy with your weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editor and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

Unlock the vast world of Apple news straight to your inbox. With coverage on everything from exciting product launches to essential software updates, this is your go-to source for the latest updates on all the best Apple content.

Our weekly newsletter is expertly crafted to immerse you in the world of streaming. Stay updated on the latest releases and our top recommendations across your favorite streaming platforms.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Earlier this year, I put Google Fi’s $35/month unlimited plan to the test against my Visible service — concluding that it’s a great alternative for anyone who wants a hassle-free setup. In my never-ending quest to find the best cheap cell phone plans, I spent the past 30 days evaluating TextNow’s $35.99/month unlimited plan to see how it holds up and what kind of value it offers over the competition.

I actually signed up for TextNow’s free talk-and-text service a couple of years ago, but it lacked the consistent data connectivity and allotment I needed access to while on the go. Now, the MVNO is stepping up its lineup with an unlimited data plan that runs $8.99 per week, or $35 per month if you want to maximize your savings — directly rivaling Google Fi's entry tier, and for a little more, Visible+ Pro.

If you’re wondering whether it’s worth switching to TextNow’s unlimited plan, I’ll break down the data allowances, extra features, and real-world performance to help you decide if it's worth getting over my experience with Visible and Google Fi.

Unlimited talk & text and 2GB data to/from Canada and Mexico

While swapping eSIMs is often an exercise in futility, the setup process with TextNow was remarkably painless. All it took to get the service running was downloading the TextNow app, logging in, and initiating the eSIM setup. Once I entered my phone's IMEI, it took just a few minutes for the eSIM profile to push directly to my device.

The app also made hardware upgrades effortless: I easily transferred the line from my Motorola Razr Fold over to the Pixel 11 Pro Fold, and it’s now active on my Vivo X500 Pro Max. If your handset doesn’t support eSIM, TextNow can still ship you a physical SIM card. Overall, I’m thrilled I didn’t run into any roadblocks when swapping between daily drivers.

Both Google Fi and Visible also offer the convenience of eSIM activation, with Fi delivering the same frictionless setup as TextNow. Visible, on the other hand, has been hit-or-miss in my experience — with one device swap leaving me stuck with technical support for several hours to get it resolved.

While it’s advertised as an all-you-can-eat offer, TextNow’s plan technically offers unlimited data to chow down on each month — but in reality, it comes with 20 GB of premium high-speed data. I’m a power user and could easily burn through that if I didn’t already have other services to lean on, but it should suffice for most people who primarily stay on Wi-Fi.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

On average, I’ve been getting about 80 Mbps down and about 4 Mbps up. These speeds aren’t a home run compared to the 200 Mbps speeds I got while running on Google Fi with ultra wideband connectivity, so you’ll have to wait a bit longer to download massive files onto your phone. Visible offers faster speeds too in my experience, but Google Fi has always been better.

It's worth mentioning the data caps with each: 20 GB for TextNow, 30 GB for Google Fi, and none technically with Visible. In the fine print details, though, Visible says that "in times of congestion, we may temporarily prioritize Heavy Data User's data behind other traffic for the remainder of the cycle." Meaning, it's unlimited data, but "Heavy Data Users" can see their speeds reduced.

In real-world situations, though, TextNow's data speeds were more than enough to stream Instagram and TikTok reels on the mass transit commute home without any buffering.

One of the biggest adjustments is having to rely on the TextNow app for all your calling and texting needs, which requires granting default app permissions during initial setup. Since I tested the service exclusively on Android devices, losing access to my default phone and messaging apps wasn’t a major issue — especially since Google Messages also routes messages over a data network.

The saving grace of being tied to the TextNow app is that the paid tier eliminates the intrusive ads that plagued the free tier when I tested it a couple of years ago. On top of that, the messaging client supports the modern features I rely on, including smooth handling of GIFs, stickers, videos, and emoji reactions.

At this price point, TextNow leaves out two perks I rely heavily on with my primary Visible line: mobile hotspot and smartwatch connectivity. Visible includes both out of the box, while Google Fi’s $35 plan covers smartwatch data.

Even though TextNow technically doesn’t support hotspot functionality on paper, I was still able to toggle the option on my phone and connect in a pinch. Over my 30 days of testing, the connection was never throttled or shut down — though there’s always a risk the carrier could clamp down on unauthorized tethering down the road.

Still, in such a competitive price bracket, it’s tough to overlook these omissions when rival carriers package them in at no extra charge. Visible+ Pro, for example, lumps in both crucial features at no extra cost.

At the same $35 monthly rate, Google Fi simply delivers a more compelling overall package — not to mention free smartwatch connectivity. Add in Fi's dual-network “Super Network” switching to help eliminate coverage dead zones, and it easily stands out as the superior pick.

Visible+ Pro is also a superb option, despite costing a little more at $39 per month. Although, that extra spread does come with welcome additions I simply don't get with either Google Fi Unlimited Essentials and TextNow Unlimited — such as mobile hotspot functionality, smartwatch data, and a robut set of international features.

While TextNow gets the job done, it functions best as a short-term bridge or secondary option. Paying monthly saves you the most money, but having the flexibility to pay $8.99 week-to-week is a solid perk if you only need temporary connectivity in a pinch.

If nothing else, TextNow’s free talk-and-text option remains well worth setting up as an emergency backup for when your primary carrier suffers an unexpected outage.
